# Verified offsets and formats

The dictionary of what we know about the game's Steam build: addresses, structures, files and rules, one line per fact. Only things confirmed **on this PC's build** go here; leads from other projects stay in the Leads section until re-verified. The full text of every entry, with each run, bench, cause and correction, is in [docs/VERIFIED_OFFSETS_HISTORY.md](docs/VERIFIED_OFFSETS_HISTORY.md): look the key up there.

Build: Steam buildid 15194255, exe SHA-256 `9002b2de6a1f91a790bd0673de125d1cf833f7942bfec827cdcf6ba64d5849ed`.

**How to read:** `exe+0x...` is an offset from the game executable's base; `actor+0x...` and the like are fields of the game's structures. 'Name in code' is the constant the DLL uses (`src/dll/`). The full old text of every entry, with each run, bench and cause, is in the history file under its key. Dates are the last verification.

**How to add an entry:** a finding is one row under its topic in section 5, `| F<n> | <finding> | <how verified> | <date> |`: one to three short sentences (300 characters at most, 600 for a field layout written as a compact list), how verified in a few words (100 at most). A lead is one bullet under its topic in section 6, `- **<title>:** (L<n>): <claim>. Source: <...>. To verify: <...>.` (450 at most, 700 for a layout), never written as confirmed. Keep every address the code names; leave the runs, numbers and story to MODLOG; keep hedges; tie one run's observation to its run. A new entry takes the highest number in the file + 1 and goes at the end of its topic; numbers never change. Cite entries by number, not title. A promoted lead becomes `Confirmed: see F<n>.`

**State of this file (2026-11-10):** sections 1 to 4 are keyed and sorted. Sections 5 (findings) and 6 (leads) are grouped by the same 18 topics, every entry in the short form above (rewritten 2026-11-10 from the full text; see 'Gaps' at the end for what's left).

### First bytes the DLL checks

Each address the DLL compares with a `k<Name>Bytes` array of its own: before hooking it (`HookFunction`), or before calling or patching it (`CodeMatches`, `SameBytes`, `BytesMatch`, `Code`, `same`, `bytesMatch`, a `memcmp`), with those bytes; a different game build fails these checks and the DLL leaves that part off. ## 3. Structures
Only the rows that were keyed by a field are here. Field facts stated inside other entries are not yet folded in: mentions in the old text per base: actor 190, player 19, save 17, weapon 15, owner 13, enemy 11, move 11, entity 9, copy 8, sheet 4, entry 2, instance 1.

## 5. Findings and game rules (by topic)
One row per finding (a game rule, structure or behaviour confirmed on this build): the finding in one to three short sentences, how it was verified in a few words, the date of the last verification. Grouped by topic, in F order within each topic; the numbers never change. Each entry's full old text, with its runs and evidence, is in the history file: find its F number in the 2026-11-10 snapshot; where that line is cut off (F1 to F319), the 2026-10-22 snapshot has the full row under the same finding text. A new finding takes the next free number (the highest in the file + 1: F481 next) and goes at the end of its topic's table.

### Actors, handles and the Sora copy

| # | Finding | How verified | Date |
|---|---|---|---|
| F2 | Object table type byte `row+4`: 0 = player class (Sora, Roxas, forms), 1 = party member (Donald, Goofy, world allies), 5 = weapon. | The Sora copy found as the one type-0 actor that isn't the list head, once per run | 2026-10-04 |
| F3 | Handles are compressed pointers (u32): bit 31 set, bits 25..30 = index into the u64[64] base table at `exe+0x2B0D720`, bits 0..24 = offset. Encode with `exe+0x4AD240(obj)`, decode with `exe+0x4AD270(h)`. | Disassembly; decoded enemy fields hit Sora's and Goofy's actors; the game's AI took our handles | 2026-10-05 |
| F8 | Sora's motion 54 is a ~22-frame collapse that loops; Sora has no motion 55, and an id Sora lacks gives a T-pose. After 54 the game sets no motion itself (Sora stays stuck) until SetMotion 0. | Bench: actor+0x180 read back after SetMotion, screenshots, stick test | 2026-10-06 |
| F64 | Our avatar packet's flags byte: 1 has actor, 2 airborne, 4 downed (lying), 8 other motion bank, 16 getting up after a down (DownedIsDown && !DownedIsLying). "Downed" stays lying-only for the lethal-hit rule. | Bench: the host held its enemies through the friend's get-up and let go when it ended | 2026-10-08 |
| F67 | The extra Sora copy (P_EX100, a second player-class actor) has character id 1 (Sora, read by `exe+0x3B5B20`), team 1, the local Sora's stat block (`actor+0x5C0` = `exe+0x2A23598`, slot 0: our Sora's HP / max) and the same Keyblade model as our Sora (`actor+0xD60`). The pause menu has no card for it. | Two-copy bench (extra copy inspect): the same in both games | 2026-10-13 |
| F68 | `exe+0x4AD270(h)` returns null for h = 0, else base table[(h & 0x7FFFFFFF) >> 25] OR (h & 0x1FFFFFF). Bit 31 is ignored and nothing is checked: a dead slot reads -1, giving address -1. | Ghidra; a crash at `exe+0x3C6161` with rax = -1 from a handle | 2026-10-07 |
| F70 | Motion-trigger dispatch `exe+0x40DD70(trigger record, motion context)` switches on the record's type byte (+2): 0x11 = cast "spell goes out" (`exe+0x3C6150`); 22 = weapons appear; 29 = `exe+0x3D6A90(owner)`, each weapon flashes and shows, only if owner+0x6C8 bit 2. Item use goes to `exe+0x3F6790`. | Ghidra + hooks on its targets fired at the expected motion frames | 2026-10-07 |
| F96 | Handle decode `exe+0x4AD270`: object = `*(u64*)(exe+0x2B0D720 + 8 × ((h & 0x7FFFFFFF) >> 25))` OR (h & 0x1FFFFFF); 0 gives 0. It can be done from outside the game. | Read-only probe: every cutscene actor handle decoded to an actor that moved with the scene | 2026-10-10 |
| F98 | Actor facing: angle a at entity +0x4C, in radians (a cutscene's rotY in degrees is the same angle). Forward = (sin a, 0, cos a), so the side direction is (cos a, 0, −sin a). | Read-only probe: a TT 707 event Sora at −100° ran along (sin, cos) of −100° | 2026-10-10 |
| F99 | Active entity list: head `exe+0x2A171C8`, next = the handle at `actor+0xA90` (decoded like any handle). `exe+0x3BFB70(actor)` tells whether an actor is in this list. | Read-only probe in TT 707: all the scene's visible actors were in the list | 2026-10-10 |
| F101 | `actor+0` = u32 handle to the actor's class. Classes: cutscene (event) actors `exe+0x752658` (the factory `exe+0x3DF930` gives it to any object asked for with bit 30, object OR 0x40000000), field Sora P_EX120 `exe+0x750300`, PRIZE `exe+0x74C988`, F_EX030_TT `exe+0x74FEC0`, F_EX000 `exe+0x752738`, TT NPCs *_RTN `exe+0x752498`, SAVE_POINT `exe+0x752580`, Keyblade W_EX010 `exe+0x74E4A8`. A scene's Sora (P_EX120) is object type 0 like the player, so the type alone doesn't tell them apart. | Read-only probe on both copies through TT 707: every cutscene actor had `exe+0x752658` | 2026-10-10 |
| F110 | `actor+0xA48` (f32 x, y, z) is a one-frame displacement treated like a push: added before the per-entity update `exe+0x3BFD30`, it moves the actor that much the same frame through the terrain collision (a wall stops it) without changing its motion. The move clears it every frame. | Two-copy bench (our spawn step); the move code from Ghidra | 2026-10-10 |
| F121 | Sora's crown setter `exe+0x3CD8A0(actor, value)` changes it on that one actor only: 0x9A copper, 0x9B silver, 0x9C gold, 0xA4 none; it works on our Sora and the Sora copy. The game's pick `exe+0x3A9E20(actor)` counts the Proofs owned and calls it; a room change runs it again and resets the crown. | One-copy bench, Merlin's House: screenshots of each value; also on a cutscene Sora | 2026-10-11 |
| F122 | The 3 items at `*(actor+0x7B0)+0x68` are `kn::TEXAnmWork` (RTTI of vtable `exe+0x5B3C48`), one per TEXA block in the model's texture entry (BAR type 0x07). P_EX100's third block (ids 0x9A-0xA4) is the crown, also in every Drive Form; costumes like `P_EX100_WI`, P_EX120 and close-up heads have none. | RTTI read from the exe; TEXA survey of the OpenKH extraction, `_WI` re-read | 2026-11-12 |
| F123 | Character model (BAR type 0x04, OpenKH ModelSkeletal): 0x90 reserved, {type, subtype, attr, u32 shadow offset}, u16 bones, u16 textures, u32 bone offset, u32 bone data offset, i32 group count, then 0x20-byte group headers {i32 attributes (bits 6-10 part, 11-15 mesh, bit 0/1 alpha), u32 texture, u32 polygons, u16, u16, u32 DMA packet offset, u32 bone matrix offset, u32 packet count, pad}; bones 0x40 each. P_EX100 group 3 = the crown: texture 2, part 1, mesh 1, 48 polygons, attributes 0x843, bone 140 only. The close-up head H_EX500 has no crown group. | Parsed the OpenKH extraction with OpenKH's ModelSkeletal layout | 2026-10-11 |
| F132 | Setting `actor+0xA08` = 0.0, `+0xA0C` = 0.0 and `+0x9B8` OR= 0x10 on the Sora copy fades it out mid-fight: gone the next frame, HUD entry dropped, Sora and the fight go on. It needs our DLL's player-removal guard; without it a Borough fight crashed. | Two copies: a Borough fight crashed twice without the guard, fine with it | 2026-10-11 |

### Scripts and their traps

| # | Finding | How verified | Date |
|---|---|---|---|
| F82 | `actor+0x5B0` = the actor's AI script VM; a script thread / VM object has its owner actor at +0x60. `exe+0x3E1C80(thread, ?)` runs a thread through the AI command runner `exe+0x41B400(thread, table, 0, 0)` and decrements owner+0x5B8 when the thread ends. The VM runs from ~20 places. | Live reads of two enemies, disassembly, a working hook on `exe+0x41B400` | 2026-10-08 |
| F139 | Script syscalls (opcode 10 of the interpreter `exe+0x41B400`): table `*(exe+0x753490 + 8*table)`, 16-byte entries {u64 fn, u32 flags: low 16 bits = argument count, bit 30 = returns a value}, called as fn(u64* args), the result in args[0]. Writing our own function into an entry's fn puts it in front of that call for every script; all 828 entries named by OpenKH's BdxTraps.cs match by flags. Arguments and results are tagged u64s, high 32 bits: 0x544E4940 '@INT', 0x544C4640 '@FLT', 0x52444140 '@ADR' (a handle, decoded with `exe+0x4AD270`; actors pass their own handle). | Bench pairs: every script call logged with names, arguments and results; no crash | 2026-10-12 |
| F140 | `exe+0x2A250B0` = the script thread being run (set by `exe+0x3E1C80` around its `exe+0x41B400` call, 0 after); `thread+0x60` = the actor whose script it is, `thread+8` = its program counter (u16 words). `actor+0x5B8` counts the actor's live threads. | Bench pairs: every logged script call named a plausible actor | 2026-10-12 |
| F163 | What some script actions change: trap_obj_set_flag / trap_obj_reset_flag(a, n) = bit n of `actor+0x120` (0 and 9 seen); trap_obj_pattern_enable(a, n) = bit n of +0xD10; trap_btlobj_set_sheet sets +0x5C0 (stat block pointer); method_obj_disable_collision(a, 0) sets +0x5AC = 1; trap_sysobj_motion_start / _change write +0x170..+0x19C (motion ids +0x180/+0x184, +0x18C, clock +0x19C); trap_obj_act_start writes +0x4A8/+0x4B0, +0x4BC, +0x5B8; trap_obj_set_movement writes +0x14/+0x1C; trap_obj_fly writes +0xC, +0x2C. trap_obj_hide_part and the stealth calls change none of the actor's 0xD50 bytes. | Bench pairs: byte diffs of the actor before and after each logged call | 2026-10-12 |
| F199 | `trap_obj_act_start(actor, name)` 1/8 = `exe+0x42C6B0` -> `exe+0x3CAE60(actor + 0x390, name)`, which finds the name in the actor's act table; the name is a string in the boss's script data. Our DLL called `exe+0x3CAE60` in the friend's game with the act name the host sent, and that Xemnas's script went on from it. Final Xemnas acts: atk_throw, atk_dance_start, mode_battle_boss (normal fight), btl_hard, atk_normal_laser1/2/3 (+ _child, _finish), btl_super_hard (desperation), atk_crowd_laser, rc_crowd_laser (barrage, its Reflect), rc_crowd_laser_finish, laser_faint (floats at 1 HP), stop. | Two-copy bench, Final Xemnas (host log of acts started); the lookup from Ghidra | 2026-10-14 |
| F254 | Vacuums: `trap_vacuum_create` 2/77 `exe+0x433200(vector handle, speed)` makes a 0x34-byte record (`exe+0x152430(0x34)`, filled like `exe+0x3F8F20`), linked into list head `exe+0x2AE7DC0` / tail `exe+0x2AE7DC8` by the handle at +0x30. ### Party, companions and Limits

| # | Finding | How verified | Date |
|---|---|---|---|
| F6 | A companion is KO'd only when the game's HP change `exe+0x3D2EB0` takes its HP to 0 (writing 0 does nothing). It stays down (motion 54) while a fight lasts and gets up (motion 55) ~6-7 s after the battle flag drops (5.7 s out of a fight) with max/4 HP; in one bench run Goofy got up with max/2 (F39). | Bench: Goofy and Donald KO'd by a debug command, HP, motion and battle flag logged | 2026-10-06 |
| F10 | Party entry `save+0x3534 + 4*world` = u8 {player, active 1, active 2, bench}; 0x03 = the world's ally (Beast in 05, Mulan in 08, Auron in 06), sometimes with 0x80 (unknown). Party menu swaps rewrite it; if the copy (0x00) takes an active slot with the bench empty, the game benches the one replaced. | User's save file (33 slots) and live patches of the entry | 2026-10-06 |
| F11 | Where the copy replaced Goofy, our DLL reports it as Goofy (id 3): the main menu lists Sora, Beast, Sora, Donald, and the party screen's cursor skips the copy's card (it can't be benched). | Bench, BC save, single copy: screenshots and log, no crash | 2026-10-06 |
| F12 | A field party swap (command menu > Party) is Sora carrying out command 0x5D or 0x5C (type 0x49); about 0.33 s later the game builds the new member, called from `exe+0x3E9299`, with the member build `exe+0x3C2FC0`. The main menu's party screen swaps through the same build with no 0x5C/0x5D command. | Two-PC runs: the host swapping by hand, and the friend in the main menu's party screen | 2026-10-12 |
| F39 | On the friend's copy of the host's companion, the game's HP change `exe+0x3D2EB0` to 0 sets `+0x9B8` bit 2 and it stays down. ISteamUserStats012 vtable offsets: GetStat float 0x8 / int32 0x10, SetStat float 0x18 / int32 0x20, GetAchievement 0x30, SetAchievement 0x38, ClearAchievement 0x40, StoreStats 0x50, IndicateAchievementProgress 0x68. | F339 | `OnFrame` runs on another thread than `OnInit`: the frame thread has a 1 MB stack reserve and no stack guarantee (0 bytes); `SetThreadStackGuarantee` in `InstallCrashLogger` covers only the init thread. | Bench `saferead` (two copies), thread log lines | 2026-10-26 |
| F340 | A vectored handler of ours first in line that answers 'continue execution' with Rip moved to a landing ends the dispatch: a fault in our read routine never reaches clr.dll's handler and the game runs on (CRASH_RISK_REVIEW section 4, way 1). | Bench `saferead` (two copies): faulting reads all landed, games alive | 2026-10-26 |

## 6. Leads (by topic)
Not yet verified on this build (Ghidra, a crash, another project): the claim, its source and what would confirm it. Same topics as section 5, in L order within each. A lead confirmed in part starts with 'Confirmed: ...' and names the finding or row. Each lead's full old text is in the history file: find its L number in the 2026-11-10 snapshot; where that bullet is cut off, the 2026-10-22 snapshot has it in full under the same title. L261 to L265 were numbered after that snapshot: they are the first five bullets of its section 6, in order. A new lead takes the next free number (the highest in the file + 1: L266 next) and goes at the end of its topic.

### Actors, handles and the Sora copy

- **Motion playing and the second motion file:** (L41): `exe+0x3C64A0` (behind `exe+0x3C63E0`, `exe+0x3C6CC0`) plays the entry on `target+0x158` and, if `target+0x6C8` bit 2 is set, on actors at `target+0xD60`, `+0xD68`. `exe+0x3C7190` plays from the 2nd file (`+0x160`, else `exe+0x3C86A0`) and sets `+0x2B8` to it, so `+0x2B8` != 0 isn't always a grab or Limit file. `trap_obj_motion_is_capture` reads `+0x18C` bit 14 after `exe+0x3BA720`. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-14. To verify: log `+0x2B8` on a bench.
- **Motion lookup and the second motion file:** (L51): 1/11 `trap_sysobj_motion_start` (`exe+0x42B320`) and 1/12 `_change` (`exe+0x42B3B0`) both end in `exe+0x3C86A0(actor+0x158, ...)`. The lookup `exe+0x3C7C50(motCtrl, motion)` tries the second file `actor+0x160` first, then `actor+0x158`. Lead: Saïx's Berserk motions may come from a file the host's script loads that the friend's Saïx (brain off) never gets. Source: Ghidra, session 34. To verify: compare both games' Saïx `+0x158..+0x2C0` at Berserk.
- **Close-ups hide the body with SeqPart:** (L72): Cutscene command 0x0D SeqPart {s16 start frame, s16 end frame, s16 actor id, s16 parts[] ending -1} is handled by `exe+0x2D1310` -> `exe+0x1A8800(*(actor+0x7B0), &parts)`. Reading of `mu_event_112`: the list = parts shown; a close-up hides part 0 (the body) and leaves part 1 (the crown) on, fitting the crown floating in a close-up. Source: script + Ghidra 2026-10-11. To verify: remove part 1 from such a list and see the floating crown stop.
- **Sora's crown comes from owned Proofs:** (L73): Owning Proofs (Connection: Lingering Will; Nonexistence: all Data Organization; Peace: Mushroom XIII) puts a crown on Sora's head on its own, not as an equipped item: 1 Proof = copper, 2 = silver, 3 = gold. Source: wikis, 2026-10-11. To verify: own 1, 2 and 3 Proofs on a bench and look at the crown.
- **Crown pick callers and the cutscene crown command:** (L74): Confirmed: the crown pick `exe+0x3A9E20`, its values and the setter `exe+0x3CD8A0`, see F121. Not seen live: the pick is tail-called per actor from `exe+0x3A7040`, `exe+0x3A8E70` and `exe+0x3A7F30` (on player `exe+0x2A105D0`); a cutscene command (handler ending at `exe+0x2D0E1B`) reads u16 cmd+8 (value) and byte cmd+0xA (1 = on `exe+0x3CD8A0`, else off `exe+0x3CD8B0`). Source: disassembly + Ghidra 2026-10-11. To verify: hook these on a bench.
- **A native Sora clone shares the local Sora's status record:** (L104): A native Sora clone shares the local Sora's status record, because the pool lookup `exe+0x3C04E0` shares records on an exact key match (Sora key 1, Roxas key 14). Relevant to 1.36 / 3.4. Source: another project (Volpestyle, `docs/NATIVE_PRIVATE_STATUS.md`). To verify: compare the status record of Sora and a clone on a bench.
- **`actor+0x120` bits 25 and 26:** (L113): On the friend, `actor+0x120` bit 26 (`0x4000000`) was on in most samples on every type seen (Shadows `M_EX020`, `M_EX530`, `M_EX710`; one brain on or off); on the host bit 25 instead now and then. Nothing visible is linked to them so far. Source: disassembly, 2026-10-13 (session 30). To verify: flip the bits on a bench and look for a change.
- **set_pos `exe+0x3B6100` is more than a place write:** (L176): `exe+0x3B6100(actor, float[4])` sets `actor+0x120` bit 21 (0x200000), calls the list check `exe+0x3BFB70` (F99; result unused), copies the vector to `+0x670` with `+0x67C` = 1.0, rebuilds the world matrix `+0x40` at once, and with `+0x6C8` bit 1 clears `+0xD24..+0xD33`. Our held stream writes `+0x670` and the facing directly instead, as world sync does. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-31 (TODO 1.144). To verify: call it on a bench and read bit 21.
- **OpenKH's objentry types:** (L177): Confirmed: types 5, 9, 10 and 15 live, see F425. OpenKH's objentry types: 0 player, 1 friend, 2 NPC (non-combat), 3 boss, 4 zako, 5 weapon, 8 field object, 9 BTLNPC (battle NPCs), 10 chest, 11 map tool, 12 world map; 2 not yet seen here. Source: another project (Volpestyle `docs/OPENKH_REFERENCE.md`, OpenKH), 2026-11-01. To verify: find a type-2 NPC in a live actor census.
- **`actor+0x180` holds two kinds of number:** (L185): The player's 0-5 are OpenKH motion ids (mset entry = id x 4 + variant: P_EX100 walk = entries 4/6/7), but a chest's 151 closed / 152 opened / 153 opening are F_EX030.mset's raw entry numbers; as ids they would need entries 604+, which don't exist. Maybe objects without the 4-variant layout use the raw index; player attacks 151-173 not checked. Source: catalog motions, 2026-11-03. To verify: read `+0x180` in a player attack.
- **A Dusk's `actor+0x180` in a fight:** (L186): In one bench run (`rc_dusk`) a Dusk hit by Goofy and Auron showed 16 / 18 / 20 / 21 most of the time (OpenKH: DAMAGE_BLOW_* / DAMAGE_TORNADO_*); 54 / 55 STUN_LOOP / STUN_END never showed. Its moves 201-206 and 234 would be EX050-EX055 and EX083 (ids or raw entries: L185). The daze after a Reversal (RC 69) is stun-like, its number not known (TODO 1.174). Source: bench log 2026-11-04. To verify: read `+0x180` in that daze.
- **OpenKH can add object table rows:** (L306): OpenKH's patcher applies a `listpatch` of type `objentry` as add or overwrite by object id and writes the rows sorted by id (`OpenKh.Patcher/PatcherProcessor.cs`), so our `mod.yml` could add a row with a new id, e.g. a renamed body model with Sora's `.mset`. Source: OpenKH source, 2026-11-12. To verify: such a row built in game (the copy's look through `formlook`).
- **The `_RTN` constructor:** (L309): `exe+0x4165E0` builds objentry type 15 (0xD00 bytes, class `exe+0x752498`, F101) and sets +0x6C8 bit 3. The base constructor `exe+0x3B3200` zeroes +0x9E8 and +0x9F0, which only a spawn group's setup (vtable+0x68) fills. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-12. To verify: read +0x9E8 of a factory-made `_RTN` on a bench.
- **An actor's serial:** (L310): `exe+0x3B5BF0(actor)` returns the actor's serial (its spawn record's +0x1E), or 0 with no record. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-12. To verify: call it on a bound enemy and a factory-made actor on a bench.
- **The ride record:** (L314): +0x9D8 u32 handle of the ridden actor (F547), +0x9E0 u64 its collision part (array at the ridden actor's +0x9C8, 0x88 each; part +0x80 the floor object matched with the rider's +0x748, +0x00/+0x40 old and new matrices). Set only by `exe+0x409E70`; cleared by `exe+0x409F30` (also clears the ridden actor's +0x7B8 bit 0x40). Source: Ghidra 2026-11-13. To verify: log +0x9E0 of a Sora on `F_WI360` on a bench.
- **The ride is rebuilt every frame and carries the rider:** (L315): the collision step `exe+0x3B9090` clears +0x9D8 each update and sets it again from +0x748 (only if +0x18C bit 6 is clear); while set, the movement step `exe+0x3B89A0` adds `exe+0x40A0A0`'s move of the part and turns the rider; no parent link. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-13. To verify: a still Sora on the shaking `F_WI360` moves with it.

### Spawning, targeting and enemy removal

- **Group spawning pace:** (L86): SpawnTask `exe+0x3A4F90` calls `exe+0x3FF000(group, player pos)` per group each frame: unless SpawnBlocked `exe+0x3ABC80`, it counts the cooldown `group+0x20` down and calls GroupTrigger `exe+0x3FE320` for each trigger box holding the position, so the spawner runs only while a position is in a box. Spawner `exe+0x3FE6F0` waits out the cooldown, makes a type-2 record's enemy only if no actor has its serial (`exe+0x3B41E0`), it isn't defeated and its stage byte <= `group+0x2C`, then sets the cooldown to the record's delay (+0x2A). Type 1 spawns at the player (`exe+0x2A105D0`). Source: Ghidra 2026-10-09 (session 20); the delay unit fits the two-PC logs. To verify: time a group's spawns.
- **Live enemies removed on the host near the friend:** (L88): In one two-PC run, live enemies of HB 04/09 group 247 (rooftops, y -1000 to -1200), triggered by the friend, were removed on the host inside our swap distance while the host's Sora was on the street (y 0). Source: two-PC log 2026-10-09 (session 20, TODO 1.45); not benched. To verify: a bench with the friend's copy on those rooftops and the host on the street.
- **Fade-out removal doesn't always dispose the enemy:** (L89): In one two-PC run the friend's Shadow (serial 87) got fade 0, speed 0 and `+0x9B8` bit 4 (F59) and kept updating minutes later. Just after, the friend's spawn sync called SpawnEntityAt `exe+0x3FE650` for that serial (FindActorBySerial `exe+0x3B41E0` returned 0) and no actor appeared; unknown which undid the removal. Source: two-PC log 2026-10-09, friend side (TODO 1.45). To verify: a bench that removes an enemy this way, then spawns its serial.
- **SetTarget modes:** (L93): SetTarget `exe+0x3BE2B0`, mode at slot +0xC (`enemy+0xC04`): 0 the player pointer `exe+0x2A105D0` (or `exe+0x40C870(exe+0x2A16370)` when actor+0x120 bit 11 is clear); 1 the last party attacker `+0xD40` if valid and not dead (`+0x9B8` bit 2), else the player; 2 `exe+0x3BEE60(slot, player pointer, 0)`; 3 the nearest target point over the list `exe+0x3DB9D0`; 4 `exe+0x3BEA30`; 5 like 3 with a bonus for actors with `+0x6C8` bit 10; 6 sight search `exe+0x3BE4B0` (best score in range wins, nothing in range = empty slot). Source: Ghidra 2026-10-08. To verify: hook `exe+0x3BE2B0` on a bench and log each mode's pick.
- **The get-target call:** (L94): `exe+0x4319D0` returns the actor's target slot; first, if the slot isn't valid (`exe+0x3BDE30`), `+0x6C8` bit 4 is set and the mode isn't 3, it re-selects with SetTarget mode 2. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-08. To verify: hook `exe+0x4319D0` on a bench and see an invalid slot re-selected.
- **Removing a live enemy through its class handler:** (L103): Confirmed: for the HB Shadows the removal goes through the fade route slot `+0x38` to `exe+0x3B4700`, see F81. Still a lead: an enemy that's alive gets removed via its class handler, and the `+0x40` predicate path (not seen on the HB Shadows). Source: another project (Volpestyle `docs/ENEMY_PARITY.md`, read 2026-10-08), for TODO 1.4. To verify: hook the class's `+0x40` slot and see it run on a removal.
- **Enemy target choice is the syscall target_search:** (L105): Enemy target choice is bdscript syscall `target_search` (bank 1, index 121, code `0x4303A0`), called through its `.data` table entry `exe+0x755B00` (table `0x755370` + 16 x 121). Source: another project (Volpestyle `docs/ENEMY_TARGET_REMOTE.md`). To verify: hook the entry on a bench and see it run when an enemy picks a target.
- **The actor factory's admission check:** (L108): The actor factory `exe+0x3DF930(objectId, pos4, yaw)` checks its own admission at `exe+0x3A1F00`: objentry `+0x54` weight <= limit `exe+0x2A0F7DC` minus used `exe+0x2A0F830`. Source: another project (Volpestyle `docs/ENEMY_PARITY.md`), relevant to 2.5 / 6.4. To verify: read the limit and used values on a bench while spawning until a spawn is refused.
- **Burrowed enemies keep their spawn links:** (L111): A burrowed enemy keeps its spawn controller at `actor+0x9E8` and its spawn record at `actor+0x9F0`; Volpestyle uses the pair to tell a re-made enemy from the same one. Source: another project (Volpestyle). To verify: read both fields on a burrowed enemy before and after it comes up on a bench.
- **The removal test of actor class `exe+0x7528E8`:** (L119): Class `exe+0x7528E8` (`B_AL110`, `B_AL120`, `M_AL020_ICEE2`), vtable `exe+0x5D2D68`: +0x38 `exe+0x419B40` (to `exe+0x3B4700`, the fade route's slot), +0x40 `exe+0x419B90` (to `exe+0x3DAC30`, the removal test), +0x48 `exe+0x419BA0` (disposal). The per-entity update `exe+0x3BFD30` calls +0x40 every frame while `+0x120` bit 28 is set, and +0x48 when it says yes, needing script threads `+0x5B8` 0, no effect records (`actor+0x80` or `actor+0x98` 0), the slot at handle `+0xBB4` gone or its `+0x14` 0. `trap_obj_leave` (1/28, `exe+0x42C810`), the same route as world sync's removal, starts the fade unless `+0x9B8` bit 4, then sets it. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-15 (session 36). To verify: hook +0x40.
- **Why a far enemy leaves:** (L166): The Shadow's script: its leave decision tests a force-leave bit 7 and a no-leave bit 2, then the distance to `sysobj_player` (the host's Sora) and culling, thresholds 1000 / 2000. F397 sees leaves from ~600 in the 1000 Heartless: their script may use other values, or culling. Source: another project (Volpestyle `docs/FORCED_RESYNC.md`, `ENEMY_TARGET_REMOTE.md`), 2026-10-30 (TODO 1.146). To verify: read the 1000 Heartless' script.
- **The 1000 Heartless pool may be kept by the mission:** (L167): The swap changed nothing (F399), so the leaves near the friend aren't (only) each enemy's distance check. On one bench the enemies that vanish stand round Sora, drawn but idle; the friend's Sora locks on but can't kill them. Leads: the far ones may get no update in the host's game, so the friend's copies stand still; the mission's spawner places new ones by the host's Sora. Source: bench 2026-10-30 (TODO 1.146). To verify: Ghidra on its spawner.
- **The crowd system is keyed on the player:** (L168): The controller update `exe+0x427D30` and `exe+0x41F830` / `0x41F420` copy the player's (`exe+0x2A105D0`) position to `exe+0x7538A0` every update. Areas: table `exe+0x2AF8410` (0x20 each, count `exe+0x2AF8AA0`, flags +0x1C): bit 0 set within 1800 of that point (squared at `exe+0x5D5740`), and on the nearest one. A member (state function `exe+0x426C30`) in an area with bit 0 and under 700 from the point tries a fighter (`exe+0x4270B0`). Quota: while 1000 minus the live fighters minus the kills (`exe+0x2AF8AB0`) is under `exe+0x2AF8AA8`. So near the friend's Sora no area turns on and no fighter is made. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-30 (TODO 1.146). To verify: read area bit 0 by the friend.
- **Who the crowd counts as the player:** (L169): The controller `exe+0x427D30` sets area bit 1 only while an entry of kind 0x44 (maybe Sora's attack) is listed and the area is in front of the local player (`exe+0x2A105D0`). The crowd kill `exe+0x427220` (from `exe+0x426C30`) needs that bit and the member in front of that player, then adds 1 to `exe+0x2AF8AB0`: only each game's own Sora knocks members away. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-30 (TODO 1.146). To verify: a knock by the friend.
- **How the crowd counts kills:** (L170): Confirmed: the layout part, see F404. Kills `exe+0x2AF8AB0` rise in the crowd kill `exe+0x427220`, in `exe+0x427C10` (the same for a given member) and in `exe+0x426600` on event 0x4B (likely a fighter's death); the area setup resets them at the first area. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-30 (TODO 1.149). To verify: log the kills on a bench through knock-aways and fighter deaths.
- **A crowd member and its states:** (L171): A member is 0x68 bytes (made in `exe+0x427570`), linked by handle at +0x24 (head at the list's +0x10). Runner `exe+0x411EA0` calls its type function (handle +0x10), which runs the state at +0x50; 0 = gone. What is drawn comes only from `exe+0x412450`: 16 bytes per member, place +0 to +8, bytes +0x14 to +0x17 (+0x15 0x80 hidden). Idle state `exe+0x426C30`: crowd kill `exe+0x427220`; within 700 of the point a fighter (`exe+0x4270B0`); the maker refuses within 400, so a member that close jumps back (state `exe+0x426FF0`): the ring round the player. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-30 (TODO 1.149). To verify: read a member's state at +0x50 on a bench.
- **The death path's helpers:** (L172): `exe+0x3B2E10(?, actor)` is a second shutdown start (a vtable slot), the same body as `exe+0x3B4700`; `exe+0x3A1520(exe+0x9ABDA0, id)` only bumps a kill tally (ids 1000-1071 and 3000-3011, capped at 9999); `exe+0x3B5B20(actor)` = the objentry's +0x4C. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-31 (TODO 1.150). To verify: hook `exe+0x3A1520` on a bench and watch the tally for a kill.
- **A crowd knock-away drops no orbs:** (L175): The knock `exe+0x427220` gives EXP and the journal tally, then the kill tally, the mission counter `exe+0x3FB2B0(1,0)` and kills `exe+0x2AF8AB0`; its flight state `exe+0x426A80` only moves and fades the member, then calls `exe+0x41EB30` (guess: makes it a fighter). No prize call, so the 1000 Heartless' orbs come from fighters' deaths only. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-31 (TODO 1.151). To verify: count orbs after knock-aways.

### Scripts and their traps

- **`trap_obj_is_star` (1/225):** (L24): `trap_obj_is_star` (1/225) = `exe+0x42BCB0`: decodes the actor (handle, then +4); 0 unless `actor+0x6C8` bit 1 is set; then the actor's class at vtable +0xB8 decides (for the player class `exe+0x3A7360`, the protection check of the hit-to-damage row). So 'star' = that bit + protected from hits. Source: disassembly from the exe file, 2026-10-17. To verify: call it for Sora with and without protection.
- **Script call table 1 entry 27 is a getter here:** (L57): OpenKH's name list calls {1, 27} `trap_area_event_set`, but its function `exe+0x42E3B0` (table `*(exe+0x753490 + 8)` = `exe+0x755370`, entry +27 x 16) only stores the current event program (word `exe+0x717010`, `kNow`+8) in args[0] as '@INT'. So the names near it may be off for this build. Source: Ghidra bytes (session 32). To verify: log that call's result next to the event program on a bench.
- **Script call table 11 not looked at:** (L60): Table 11 of `exe+0x753490` points to `exe+0x2B047D0`, empty in the file (filled at run time?); not in OpenKH's list, not looked at. Source: the exe file, session 29. To verify: read `exe+0x2B047D0` in a running game.
- **Script threads and magic threads:** (L99): Confirmed: the running thread and its actor, see F140. `exe+0x3E1C80(thread, parent)` runs a thread then its children (`+0x48` first, `+0x50` last, next sibling handle `+0x44`), `exe+0x2A25048` = the parent while it runs. `exe+0x3E1970(parent, 0, pc, args, n)` makes a 0x70-byte thread from the parent's program `+0`, globals `+0x58` and owner actor `+0x60`; `+8` pc, `+0x38` bit 1 = ended. `trap_magic_start_thread` (2/20, `exe+0x432CD0`) starts a thread at `pc` of the running script on a global list (head `exe+0x2A23A70`, tail `exe+0x2A23A78`), so it runs apart from its owner's brain. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-12. To verify: walk that list on a bench during a spell.
- **More script calls: effects, rotation, shadow, texture animation:** (L116): Tables `exe+0x753490`, entries {fn, flags}. Effect setters 0/38 set_pos `exe+0x41DF80`, 0/39 scale, 0/40 rot, 0/41 dir, 0/67 color copy a script vector into the effect's part (effect+0x30); 0/66 pause = effect+0xC bit 21. 1/87 `obj_effect_start_bind` `exe+0x42D540`; 1/22 `obj_effect_start_pos` = `exe+0x3B66C0(actor, id, place, a, b)`, effect encoded with `exe+0x4AD240`. 8/2 `obj_set_xyzrot` = `exe+0x3B65F0`: actor+0x680 = vec, +0x68C = vec.y, +0x9B8 bit 3 on. 6/45 `obj_hide_shadow` sets 0x200 in actor+0x124; 6/64 `obj_show_shadow` = `exe+0x3CD820`. Texture animation start `exe+0x3CD8A0` on actor+0x7B0 (also the crown's switch). Source: Ghidra 2026-10-15. To verify: call set_pos on a bench.
- **The actor's script context `actor+0x390`:** (L173): `+0x108` owner record (its `+4` = the actor's handle), `+0x110` label table (128 entries of 32 bytes, `+0` = handle of the name), `+0x118` / `+0x120` the running part, `+0x12C` bit 0 = no label jumps. `exe+0x3CA700(table, name)` finds a label, `exe+0x3CAE60(ctx, name)` jumps to it, `exe+0x3CAEC0` stops the running part (never clears `+0x110`). `exe+0x3E1A00(root)` only marks the root thread (`actor+0x5B0`) ended; it is freed later. Guess: the label table lives with that thread, so once it ends `+0x110` dangles (F403, F407). Handles: `exe+0x4AD3F0(h)` = `exe+0x2B0D720[(h & 0x7FFFFFFF) >> 25]` OR `(h & 0x1FFFFFF)`. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-31. To verify: read `+0x110` after the root ends.
- **Frame trigger type 7 runs the actor's script callback:** (L187): In the frame trigger dispatch `exe+0x40DD70` (type = record +2), case 7 calls `exe+0x3E1840(actor+0x5B0, 0x11, {actor handle, u16 +4, u16 +6}, 3, 0)` for the actor playing the motion, with no brain or bound check; case 1 (effect start) runs key 0x10 (`OBJ_ANMATR_EFFECT`) the same way with the new effect. OpenKH's anb.md calls type 7 'Game effect': wrong on this build. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-06 (TODO 1.113). To verify: break on case 7 on a bench.
- **`exe+0x3E1840` runs a script entry at once:** (L188): `exe+0x3E1840(vm, key, args, n, parent)` finds the entry with `exe+0x41C690(vm, key)`, builds a thread on the stack, runs it through `exe+0x3E1C80` and drops it: no `exe+0x3E1970` thread, so our `HookMakeThread` never sees these calls. `exe+0x3E1790(thread, key, args, n)` points an existing thread at an entry; `exe+0x3D9A60` / `exe+0x3D9AE0` start key 0x16 as a new thread. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-06. To verify: hook `exe+0x3E1840` on a bench.
- **41 motion sets have type-7 frame triggers:** (L189): 26 `BOSS` / `L_BOSS` sets (both Xemnas fights, Axel, Demyx, Xigbar, Saïx, Larxene, Lingering Will, Pete, Sephiroth...), 11 `ZAKO` / `L_ZAKO` (Demyx's water forms, Assassin, Lance Soldier...), 2 summons, 1 player Limit set, 1 battle NPC. Each can run its own script in the friend's game with its brain off. Source: catalog `callbacks.json` + OpenKH `MotionTrigger`, 2026-11-06. To verify: log type-7 calls in the friend's game.
- **What the type-7 callbacks call:** (L190): The 41 sets (L189) serve 60 models; 52 have the `OBJ_ANMATR_CALLBACK` entry: 31 start magic threads (2/20), 9 make attacks, 8 make objects; only both Final Xemnas fights capture and hook (1/29, 1/112), Barbossa's `B_CA010` / `B_CA030` attach (1/18). Of the 77 calls used, the world mirror sends 17 and grab sync handles 6, none of those three kinds. Static walk only. Source: OpenKH Bar + Bdxio, 2026-11-06. To verify: log them in a fight.
- **`trap_obj_is_ride`:** (L312): Confirmed: see F547.
- **`trap_obj_hook`'s thread:** (L316): `exe+0x42CA20` makes a thread at the given pc with the hooked actor's handle as its argument, stores it in the actor's +0xD48 (`exe+0x3D76D0`) with the running thread's owner at +0x60; each player tick `exe+0x3A89A0` runs it through `exe+0x3D7280` unless +0x18C bit 18 is set. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-13. To verify: a hooked Sora's +0xD48 back at 0 when its thread ends.
- **The scaffold's script:** (L317): Confirmed: the shake's hook launches 'the player' riding it and plays motion 3, once a flight, see F548. Its hook needs the player not invincible, not captured, riding and not in motion 3; the jump is `(0, 1000, 0, 1)`; command 364 hooks the event's user (141), captured in 252. Source: `F_WI360` decoded with OpenKH Bdxio, 2026-11-13. To verify: a launched Sora's speed on a bench.
- **Air Slash with the copy as the scaffold's player:** (L318): in one two-copy bench the host's game faulted at `exe+0x3BDAAB` in `trap_target_dup` (1/131, `F_WI360` pc 179) as the host's own Air Slash began while our DLL named the friend's copy the scaffold's player; fixed in our DLL. Source: crash, two-copy bench 2026-11-13. To verify: Ghidra `exe+0x3BDAAB` and what it reads from the copy.

### Hits, damage, spells and stats

- **A hit's damage and stat channel:** (L3): A hit reaches the stat change through `exe+0x3D3BA0(actor, hit)`: damage = hit +0x28, stat channel = hit byte +0x25. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-22 (TODO 1.112), matching a host log. To verify: read hit +0x28 and +0x25 inside `exe+0x3D3BA0` on a bench.
- **Party HP regen timer:** (L22): `exe+0x3C3150` counts `exe+0x2A239C0` down by the frame time; at 0, party places 1-3 not in the field (type not one of the field slots `exe+0x2A239B0`/`B8`) get +1 HP through `exe+0x3C0C30` when their block +0x1E0 bit 4 is set, and the timer restarts from `*(exe+0x2AE5760)+0x168`. Possibly the +1s on the friend's companion copy, not checked. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-21 (session 39). To verify: watch the timer and a member's HP on a bench.
- **What can make a player read 'star':** (L25): The player class check `exe+0x3A7360(class, actor)` is true when the handle at +4 of the scripts' team block 0x10002 (2/21 `trap_teamwork_alloc`) names a live object, whichever Sora is asked, so it would make every player 'star'. Otherwise `exe+0x3D65A0(actor)`, which includes +0xD70 > 0, a star timer set by 1/91 `trap_obj_star`. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-18 (TODO 1.94). To verify: log the team block and `exe+0x3AC260()` at a 'star' read.
- **How the game decides a hit lands:** (L33): The hit pass `exe+0x3CFF40` asks `exe+0x3D2060(attack, victim)` for each live attack: not if the victim already has this attack (`victim+0xC98`) or is its owner (attack +0x10, +0x14); only if attack +0x39 has the victim's team bit (`victim+0x4DC`). A touch of its body shapes (`exe+0x3C94C0`) runs ResolveHit `exe+0x3D1730`. No 'invincible' flag seen on this path yet. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-16. To verify: read the friend's body shapes in a Limit.
- **How a landed hit becomes damage, protection and survive rules:** (L34): Confirmed: hit apply, raw damage, the Second Chance / Once More bits, +0x126 bit 0 during a Limit (Reflect sets none), see the table rows. OnHit `exe+0x3D60C0`: a protected victim (class via `exe+0x4AD270`; player `exe+0x3A7360` uses `exe+0x3D3570`) drops the hit unless kind 5-6. `exe+0x3EE9B0` remaps the reaction by status, `exe+0x3D3BA0` applies damage; a lethal hit keeps 1 HP if class vtable +0xA8 agrees (likely Second Chance, or Once More via `exe+0x3D4880`); then `exe+0x3D2EB0` (our ApplyStatDelta hook); `exe+0x3CE980` plays the reaction. A forwarded hit calls `exe+0x3D2EB0` directly: none of these rules use the friend's own Sora. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-16. To verify: Guard's bit.
- **The player's hit reaction:** (L35): After the damage `exe+0x3D60C0` sets a flag for the actor in the 3-entry table `exe+0xABCFE8` (flags at `exe+0xABD000`, likely the party HUD portrait), then calls class vtable +0xC8 (player `exe+0x3A73D0` -> `exe+0x3D2FE0(actor, hit)`, the body's reaction, skipped when hit+0x18 bit 0). By `exe+0x3CE980(hit+0x26)`: 1 -> `exe+0x3D4960`; 3 flinch; 4 knockback, motion 0x12 / 0x10 by hit direction vs facing plus a push `exe+0x3B6420`; 0xE big knockback, motion 0x18 / 0x17. Hit +0 is its 16-byte direction. `exe+0x3CEAB0(hit)` and `exe+0x3CEA00` give further values (not read yet). Source: Ghidra 2026-10-16 (session 37, TODO 1.79). To verify: call it for a forwarded hit with these fields.
- **Who the yellow target measures from:** (L36): The lock-on record is the field command menu +0xBE8 (F176); its player is menu +0xC20 (`exe+0x3DD280` reads `*(lock+0x38)`), not +0xC38. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-16 (session 37). To verify: read menu +0xC20 in both games during a fight.
- **The knockback of a hit:** (L40): Confirmed: ResolveHit `exe+0x3D1730` runs on a landed attack, see F276. It runs `exe+0x3D1B40` (likely the knockback) before OnHit; the atkp entry (attack +0x30) has a knockback strength; the attack's vtable slot 2 `exe+0x3D1430` builds the hit direction. Scripts can land one: `trap_attack_new(actor, atkp id, -1, 0)` + `trap_attack_strike(attack, target, 0, 0)`. Source: OpenKH, Volpestyle, bench 2026-10-14. To verify: change a knockback strength.
- **The stat change's twin:** (L59): `exe+0x3D2C50(self, actor, delta, channel, flag)` has the same body as ApplyStatDelta `exe+0x3D2EB0` and is slot +0xE8 of `YS::BTLOBJ`'s function table (`exe+0x5C6250`); a hit through it would skip our ApplyStatDelta hook (claims, god mode). A trace hook never saw it called in bench fights (melee, Wisdom Form, a Limit, a summon, spells). Source: Ghidra + trace (session 31). To verify: find who calls it.
- **Battle flag right after a pause:** (L68): In one Borough fight (battle `exe+0x2A11404` = 1 just before), the first frame after our pause screen closed read 0, so the room reload, which then refused in a fight, went ahead. Source: a log, inferred, 2026-10-11 (session 27). To verify: watch `exe+0x2A11404` across a pause in a fight.
- **Hits by anyone in the friend's game count as the friend's:** (L95): Our world_sync.cpp WorldSyncBlocksDamage sends a claim for every HP change on a bound enemy, whoever made it (TODO 1.40). Source: two-PC log 2026-10-08. To verify: log the attacker of each claimed hit in the friend's game.
- **Casting a spell:** (L100): Confirmed: the cast on the copy, its list and its trigger, see F160. `exe+0x3C5170(actor, spell id, flag)` finds the spell's slot in the table `exe+0x2A23A80` (6 slots of 0x50 bytes: +8 script program, +0x18 effect instance, +0x48 data), sets the cast motion (`exe+0x3C86A0(actor+0x158, motion)`), makes the spell's thread, puts it on the magic thread list (head `exe+0x2A23A70`) and calls script entry 0x14. Only caller `exe+0x3A9DD0(actor, spell)`, called by `exe+0x3D6FC0` for a type-2 command. `exe+0x3E1970(parent, 0, pc, args, n)` makes a lasting thread (`trap_magic_start_thread`). Source: Ghidra 2026-10-12 (session 29, TODO 1.31). To verify: hook `exe+0x3C5170` while a Sora casts.
- **Attacks:** (L101): `YS::ATTACK` (`exe+0x3CF830`): +8 flags (bit 6 = no hits in the hit pass, bit 11 = to delete, 0x400 = id given), +0xC / +0x10 / +0x94 the owner's handle, +0x14 a second actor's, +0x18/+0x1C ids, +0x20 group, +0x28 effect instance handle, +0x30 its data (+4 type: 5-6 hit any team), +0x39 the teams it can hit (bit = team). `exe+0x3D2060` also skips the owner and the second actor. Attack list head `exe+0x2A24B40`, next = handle at +0xC4. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-12 (session 29). To verify: read a live attack's fields on a bench.
- **Atkp revenge damage (OpenKH):** (L109): In `00battle.bin` an atkp (attack) entry is 48 bytes; offset 42 u8 is 'Revenge damage', how much revenge value a hit adds. Source: OpenKH docs (openkh.dev 00battle), read 2026-10-08. To verify: change offset 42 and watch an enemy's revenge on a bench.
- **Fault `exe+0x3D26A5`: a motion attack whose owner handle no longer decodes:** (L114): Motion trigger `exe+0x40D810` types 10 and 0x21 reach (via the class's vtable +0x98) `exe+0x3D25D0(actor+0xC10, ...)` (kMotionAttack, hooked in motion_mirror.cpp), which then writes `decode(*(u32*)set)+0xCF0 = 0` unchecked. Seen in the friend's game while companions were sent away (a Game Over, a Valor Drive). Source: Ghidra + bench and two-PC crashes 2026-10-21 / 2026-10-29 (TODO 1.132). To verify: log the set's handle at the fault.
- **Script prizes and damage sheets:** (L118): Prize calls: 1/92 `trap_obj_scatter_prize` -> `exe+0x3B6170(actor, id)`; 1/278 `_tr` and place call 1/279 `_tr` -> `exe+0x42A980(place, n)`; 6/1 `trap_prize_appear` -> `exe+0x3DC170(place, id)`; 1/342 `_num` -> `exe+0x3DC3D0(place, id, n)`; 1/363 `_xaldin` -> `exe+0x42AD40(place, n)`. Damage sheet `*(actor+0x5C0)`: HP +0, max +4, +0x268 owner handle, +0x270 base table handle. 1/311 `trap_sheet_set_element_rate(sheet, element, rate)` -> `exe+0x3C2650`: byte sheet+0x1A8+element = base (u16 at table +0x48 + element*2) * rate / 100, rounded up. 2/9 `trap_btlobj_set_sheet(obj, id, x)` gives an actor a new sheet. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-15 (session 36). To verify: log these calls in a boss fight.
- **Sora's stat sheet is built from the save's record:** (L121): `exe+0x3C1D90(sheet, record)` fills the sheet from the 0x114-byte record at `exe+0x9ABDA0 + 0x114 x (index-1)` (= save+0x24F0): +0/+2 the two weapon items, +4/+5 HP, +6/+7 MP, +8 AP, +9/+0xA/+0xB strength/magic/defense, +0xC/+0xD two gauges, +0xF level (the level table adds to strength/magic/defense), +0x10/+0x11 armor/accessory counts with item lists at +0x14/+0x24, +0x54 80 abilities (top bit = equipped). Source: Ghidra 2026-10-23 (session 41, TODO 3.16). To verify: read the live record and sheet side by side.
- **Stat sheet fields:** (L122): +0x188/+0x18A/+0x18C/+0x18E strength/magic/defense/AP with gear, +0x190.. the same without; +0x1A8..+0x1AE resistances in percent (physical, then elements; +0x1AE all), 100 at start; +0x1D0 the ability block (`exe+0x401730(block, ability)` sets one and adds its derived values); +0x250 record, +0x254 form record, +0x26C the save part with the Drive gauge (+0x1038..+0x1043). Source: Ghidra 2026-10-23 (session 41). To verify: read a live sheet on a bench.
- **Equipment items on the sheet:** (L123): `exe+0x3C1190(sheet, item)` looks up the item's 03system equipment row (`exe+0x3E39A0`): +4..+7 add to strength/magic/defense/AP, +8..+0xE scale the resistances (x/100, rounded up), +2 is its ability. It runs for both weapon items, the form record's item, the armors and the accessories. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-23 (session 41). To verify: equip an item and compare the live sheet.
- **The sheet writes back to the save:** (L124): `exe+0x3C2120(sheet)` writes HP, MP and the two gauges into the record at +0x250 and the Drive gauge into +0x26C's +0x1038..+0x1043; `exe+0x3D6850(actor)` runs this commit, then `exe+0x3C1D90`. A private sheet for the copy must point +0x250/+0x254/+0x26C at its own buffers, or it writes our save. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-23 (session 41). To verify: watch the save record after `exe+0x3D6850` on a bench.
- **Making a stat sheet:** (L125): `exe+0x3C0620(charId, form)` (used by the player constructor) takes the shared sheet from `exe+0x3C04E0(charId)` (one per id), sets +0x26C and +0x254 for ids 1 and 14, then runs `exe+0x3C1D90`; `exe+0x3C03F0(charId, form)` always takes a new pool sheet. Handles (`exe+0x4AD240`) take any pointer (64 regions of 32 MB, table `exe+0x2B0D720`). Source: Ghidra 2026-10-23, Volpestyle agrees. To verify: make a sheet with `exe+0x3C03F0` and read it.
- **A hit's damage comes from the victim's sheet:** (L126): The hit builders set hit+0x28 = `exe+0x3C12F0(victim sheet, attacker, attack, stat)`: the attacker's strength (+0x188) or magic (+0x18A) minus the victim's defense (+0x18C), times the victim's resistance, with difficulty and low-HP factors. So in the host's game a hit on the copy uses the host's defense, armor and HP. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-23 (session 41). To verify: compare a hit on the copy with the host's sheet on a bench.
- **Prize pickup and the prize list:** (L174): Confirmed: the walk and +0x28 bit 0, see F408. Prizes are walked from `*(*(exe+0x2A24E40)+0xBF8)`: first at that pool's +0x10, next = the handle at prize +0x24. Prize +0x00 place, +0x14 u8 type, +0x28 flags (bit 0 = can be picked up, bit 3 = goes to `*(exe+0x2A105D0)` once +0x40 > 2), +0x50 pointer whose +4 f32 is added to the height. Pickup callbacks `exe+0x3DBEA0` / `exe+0x3DC7C0` skip a party member with +0x120 & 0x10080000, +0x9B8 bit 2, or (+0x6C8 bit 7 clear) prize types 7-8; `exe+0x413650(prize, member, ?)` collects. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-31 (TODO 1.151). To verify: the callbacks' skips on a bench.
- **A spell's variant depends on the caster's form:** (L301): Confirmed: +10 / +30 for `+0xDE0` 2 / 5 with `+0x6C8` bit 7 set and +1 for the finisher, see F514, F517. Lead: the base is level x 2 - 2, and 4 (Master) adds 20; `exe+0x3C6150` (entry 0x15) reads neither field. Source: Ghidra, 2026-11-12. To verify: a Master cast replayed on the copy.

### RCs and grabs

- **A held player runs its holder's step:** (L20): `exe+0x3D7280(actor)`: if `actor+0xD48` (the holder, set by `trap_obj_hook`) is set and +0x18C bit 18 is clear, it calls `exe+0x3E1C80(holder, 0)` and, on 1, `exe+0x3D6F80(actor)`. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-21 (session 39). To verify: hook `exe+0x3D7280` while a script holds a Sora.
- **The friend's RC 492 on Data Xigbar faults in the host's game:** (L28): In the host's game, the friend's RC 492 on `B_EX140_LV99` faults in script call `exe+0x432800`, which turns args[0] into an object (`exe+0x4AD270`) and returns the handle of the object at its +0x114 (`exe+0x4AD240`): the handle Xigbar's script passed named no object in the host's game. Guess, not checked: the friend's game offered 492 for its own copy of a projectile. Source: two-PC log + disassembly 2026-10-17. To verify: log args[0] there.
- **How the game offers an RC in slot 1, and Data Xigbar's chained sniper RCs:** (L29): Slot update `exe+0x4081C0` offers player+0x638 while +0x18C bit 20, and the per-enemy list; an entry `exe+0x3B68B0(cmd)` with flag bit 6 is a chain, offered only while +0x18C bit 21 and +0x638 = entry +0x22. Motion trigger `exe+0x40D810` type 0x14 offers into slot 1 of `exe+0x2A105D0`'s menu (`exe+0x3B25C0` when the actor is that player) and sets +0x18C bit 17 and +0x638; 0x19 / 0x1A set bit 20 / 21 and +0x638. `B_EX140.mset` 0x1A: entry 252 chains 491, 254 chains 492, 255 chains 491. Target info (`exe+0x3BDA50` / `exe+0x3BEE60`) = {actor handle, part, handle, -1}. Source: Ghidra + B_EX140.mset 2026-10-17 (TODO 1.83 / 1.85). To verify: why the friend's game offers only the first 598.
- **Attacks can carry an RC:** (L30): After the per-enemy loop `exe+0x4081C0` calls `exe+0x407B40(state)`, which walks the attack list: head `exe+0x2A24B40`, next = handle at +0xC4, only those with +8 bit 8 set. Its target info matches the host's Data Xigbar 598s; that it causes the 492 fault is not checked live. Player shape `exe+0x3C9C00`, body `exe+0x3B5650(player, 1, -1)`. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-17 (TODO 1.83, 1.85). To verify: log what `exe+0x407B40` offers in the sniper round.
- **A grab of a Sora in a Drive Form isn't ended by the let go:** (L42): In one two-PC run (Data Roxas, 0x12/0x15), the let go didn't end the host's grab that grab sync ran on the friend's Sora as the form body (form 3): capture 254 with the grabber's motion file, attach, camera, screens, let go + detach. Source: two-PC logs 2026-10-14. To verify: the same grab on a Sora in a Drive Form on a bench, reading its motion after the let go.
- **TWTNW RCs with one brain:** (L44): The cylinder jumps (0x12/0x1A `F_EH080` RC 612, scenes 're_f_eh080-00' to '-30'; 0x12/0x19 `F_EH100` 643) come from each game's own per-enemy list, as Xaldin's 649 do. Source: two-PC logs 2026-10-14 (session 35); fixes not yet tried. To verify: log which game offers 612 and 643 on a two-PC run.
- **The per-enemy RC list the player's menu reads every frame:** (L47): `exe+0x4081C0(slot state)` runs for the player each frame: it tries the player's RC slot, then every actor (`exe+0x407DE0`, `exe+0x407B40`); a slot fills only if `exe+0x3B6A10(row, player)` passes. Enemies add entries with `exe+0x40A590`: 1/49 `method_obj_enable_reaction_command` (`actor+0x540`) and motion trigger type 6 (7 removes); trigger 0x14 offers a command to the player. Source: Ghidra. To verify: which Xaldin's 649 and Luxord's 458 use.
- **Grab calls: what the script calls do:** (L48): Table 1 = `exe+0x755370`. An actor argument is a handle to an object whose +4 is the actor's handle. 1/29 `trap_obj_motion_capture(grabber, actor, motion, float blend)` = `exe+0x42D160` -> `exe+0x3C6CC0(grabber+0x158, ...)`: `actor+0x2B8` = grabber's motion file, `+0x180` = motion. 1/101 `trap_obj_motion_is_capture` = `actor+0x18C` bit 14. 1/18 `trap_obj_attach` `exe+0x42C550` (the first is attached to the second). 1/102 `trap_obj_detach` `exe+0x42C5F0`. 1/220 `trap_obj_move_to_space` `exe+0x42BBB0` -> `exe+0x3B9AC0` (flag: low 32 bits only). 1/112 `trap_obj_hook(actor, pc, owner)` `exe+0x42CA20`: a script thread at pc, kept in `actor+0xD48`. Source: Ghidra. To verify: log them in a grab.
- **Grab motion triggers:** (L50): 1/151 `trap_obj_motion_check_trigger(actor, n)` = `exe+0x42D230` reads bit n-1 of the byte actor+0x2AD; a grab trace saw it 0, so it was not seen set yet. Source: Ghidra (session 34). To verify: read actor+0x2AD while a grab motion runs its triggers.
- **Where an RC offer goes:** (L53): `trap_command_override_top` (script call 1/237) = `exe+0x4300A0` writes the four commands into the menu of the global player, `exe+0x3B2910(*(*(exe+0x2A105D0) + 0xDD0), ids, source)` (the shared field command menu), not into the menu of the Sora the boss grabbed. Source: Ghidra (session 34). To verify: read both Soras' menus while a boss offers its commands.
- **The current RC id (Volpestyle):** (L106): The current RC id is u16 `exe+0x2A11162` (read 0x20 with 'Open' showing at a chest, 0 elsewhere, a stale 0x37 at boot); KH2Lib's `0x2A110E2` stays 0 on Steam. Source: Volpestyle `docs/DOWNED_REVIVE.md` (for 1.26). To verify: read `exe+0x2A11162` at a chest on this build.
- **The friend's RC 635 on Final Xemnas faults in the host's run:** (L129): The host's run of the friend's attack-carried RC 635 on `B_EX170_LAST` failed with an exception at `exe+0x432810` in trap_attack_rc_receiver (adr 0x0), after the use named our attack; the game went on. Likely the attack had ended before the run (TODO 1.83's fault site). Source: two-PC run 2026-10-24. To verify: check the attack still lives when the host runs the press.
- **Data Xigbar's bouncing shots in the friend's game are its own:** (L130): In two-PC logs no copy of ours was made or bound for Xigbar's shots; their hits on the friend's Sora came as local hits and were dropped. The friend's press of their RC 492 is held back and sent to the host (reaction_sync.cpp), so its own boss never gets event 0xB and likely never ends the shot: the bullets stay visible and loud. What ends a shot after 492 is unknown. Source: two-PC logs 2026-10-24. To verify: find that call in the host's script.
- **trap_obj_hitback, the push in Shan-Yu's clash:** (L136): On each press of RC 270 the host's B_MU100 script calls trap_obj_hitback (1/146, `exe+0x42B720`) on the held Sora copy with a vector and 100; grab sync doesn't forward it. It runs `exe+0x3D4960(actor, float[4], power)`: normalized direction into actor+0xD24, speed `(power + *(actor+0x5C0)+0x1C8) * *(exe+0x6239F8)` into actor+0xD30. Source: Ghidra + two-copy logs 2026-10-25 (TODO 1.62). To verify: forward it and see the friend's Sora pushed.
- **The tornado takes a Sora through its attack's hit:** (L144): F_MU070 keeps an attack 800 against team 1 and took the copy with `trap_obj_hook` the next frame, with no hit applied (the hit handler stopped before `exe+0x3D3BA0`). Every attack-actor pair passes kCanHit `exe+0x3D2060` first, so returning 0 there for the copy should stop the grab. Source: logs + Ghidra 2026-10-25 (session 44, TODO 1.126). To verify: a bench with the copy walking into a tornado.
- **A fault putting the host's RC offer in our menu as our player changes:** (L159): a fault (0xC0000005) at `exe+0x430067`, reading +0xDD0 through a null player, in reaction_sync's offer put as our game changed its player: in one bench at a soft reset (2026-10-29), in one as the friend's Sora became PRIZE at a Game Over (2026-11-13); caught, the offer dropped. Source: bench logs. To verify: an offer during a Game Over on a bench.
- **The friend's Guard (RC 35) on a Large Body crashed the host:** (L164): Confirmed: the cause, see F416 (the script copies its user's target slot `+0xBF8`). The host ran the friend's RC 35 (Guard) on `M_EX050`, copy as user: at pc 215 `trap_obj_motion_capture` got a grabber naming no live actor, and the host crashed reading 0x158 at `exe+0x3C7C6F`. In one run the host's own 35 grabbed with `M_EX050` itself, no `trap_obj_hook`. Source: two-PC logs 2026-10-30/31 (TODO 1.142). To verify: the friend's Guard, no crash.
- **What the event RC and dialogue choice pass to `exe+0x3AABE0`:** (L182): Confirmed: the RC and choice arguments, see the `exe+0x3BCFBB` row. `exe+0x3AABE0` sends ids 0x13, 0x66-0x75, 0x83-0x85, 0x8A to `exe+0x1518E0` and 0x16, 0x46, 0x4F, 0x57-0x59, 0x62, 0x64, 0x65, 0x76, 0x81 to `exe+0x2E4570`, then calls `exe+0x3F4980`, `exe+0x3B4270` and `exe+0x3F60D0`. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-02 (TODO 5.4). To verify: hook both handlers and press an event RC.
- **Cerberus's grab shots carry no Tag:** (L269): the base matrix doesn't stay identity: the four shots take Cerberus's pose one frame in (F491). Still from the file decode only: none of `B_HE020`'s shot events 00/10/20/30 has a 0x51 tag, the only way Ghidra found to the re-aim `exe+0x406A90`, so something else sets the base; the four are one camera track cut in four. Source: Ghidra and OpenKH event decode 2026-11-10, corrected 2026-11-11. To verify: what writes event+0x60 one frame in.
- **When trap_camera_reset ends a scene camera:** (L270): 1/295 = `exe+0x430B60` -> `exe+0x165A40` only sets camera+0x41 = 1, and in F485 it ended nothing, while F186 saw it end the Twin Lords' camera (perhaps after their shot). Source: Ghidra 2026-11-10 (TODO 1.179). To verify: a reset mid-shot on a bench, watching the event block.
- **What `exe+0x3D3570` checks:** (L293): Confirmed: `exe+0x2A10500` is the mission lock word, see F504. Lead: an actor is can't-be-hit when `+0x120` bit 8, `+0x18C` bit 22, `+0x64C` bit 2, `+0x120 & 0x10080000`, `+0x9B8 & 0x210`, a room load waiting (`exe+0x9BA928`) or any bit of the word `exe+0x2A10500` (cleared with +4 and +8 by `exe+0x3A57D0`). Source: Ghidra, 2026-11-11. To verify: read these on the friend's Sora stuck after RC 307 (F501).
- **Move to space on the copy:** (L294): in the host's game the copy moved 66 and 123 units at RC 307's `trap_obj_move_to_space`, each player's own Sora 200 to 600 (F502). Likely the actor's own update makes the move (the call sets `+0x9B8` bit 12, F149) and the puppet copy doesn't run it, or our puppet write wins. Source: two-PC log, 2026-11-11. To verify: read the copy's place and `+0x9B8` for a few frames after the call.
- **What `trap_obj_move_to_space(actor, r, flag)` does:** (L295): `exe+0x3B9AC0` grows the actor's collision shape (`+0x6D0`) step by step up to r against the room (`exe+0x16FEC0`) and moves the actor (`exe+0x3B6510`) by the push-out: it clears r units around it, not a set spot. Fits Shan-Yu's clash (r 500: 0 or 32 units) and RC 307 (r 800: 200-600, F502). Source: Ghidra and logs, 2026-11-11. To verify: read `+0x70` before and after, near a wall.
- **When the movement update clears bits 26 and 27:** (L296): `exe+0x3B9090` clears `+0x120` bit 26 at a ground-to-air step and bit 27 at an air-to-ground step, sends the script event (1 fall, 2 land) and thread event 0xD / 0xE, and runs the default fall / land handlers only if the bit is still clear; it skips all this while `+0x6A0` holds a parent. Source: Ghidra, 2026-11-11. To verify: log `+0x120` around a jump.
- **`HE08_MS107`'s mission script:** (L297): signal 4 sets state 2 (dead boss), signal 74 state 3 (failed), each with `mission_lock` if not locked; UNIT_MAIN runs that one ending, then halts. No direct `exe+0x3AABE0(0x4A)` exists; Pete's, Hercules' and Megara's scripts send no signal (the boulders send 96). Source: OpenKH Bdxio decode, exe scan, 2026-11-11 (pcs match F505). To verify: log what fires 74 at RC 307's end.
- **Air Slash's part is on only in the shake:** (L313): `F_WI360.mset` motion 232 has range triggers 0x0A [865, group 1] and 0x04 [group 2], so Air Slash's part is probably collision group 2, on only while the scaffold shakes. Source: our mset parsers on the local file, 2026-11-12. To verify: read a Sora's RC list in the air beside the shaking scaffold.

### Party, companions and Limits

- **A party member's leave `exe+0x400990(member, fade)`:** (L19): For a valid member (`exe+0x3BA720`) without +0x9B8 bit 4, `exe+0x400990` starts a fade on member+0xA08 and sets that bit; it always appends the place (`exe+0x3E31C0(charId)`) to the bring-back list `exe+0x2AE9788` (count `exe+0x2AE9790`). It doesn't remove the actor: in a two-PC run it ran for the friend's Goofy during the host's Valor Form, yet Goofy stayed (TODO 1.103). Source: Ghidra and two-PC run 2026-10-21. To verify: the list after a call.
- **Which Limits the command menu lists:** (L32): The menu build `exe+0x3F29E0` fills the Limit list from the `limt` table `*(exe+0x2AE5690)` (+1..+3 characters needed, +0x2A ability). `exe+0x3E81A0(entry)` lists one when each character is found by `exe+0x3D5C70` (by `exe+0x3B5B20`) and live (`exe+0x3BA720`) and the first has the ability; greyed (state 5) unless `exe+0x3E75D0` and `exe+0x3E7E10`. The menu state `exe+0x3D88E0` and the Limit start use the same check. Our GetFriend(1) hook gives the copy when party slot 1 is empty. Drive gate `exe+0x4005E0`: form 1 needs character 3, 2 needs 2, 4-6 every slot; likely character 1 = Sora, 2 = Donald, 3 = Goofy. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-16 (TODO 1.78). To verify: what character the copy answers.
- **A Goofy Limit's file not found in the host's game:** (L39): The friend's Limit with Goofy (`P_EX030` its partner) had 255 entries at `exe+0x1B9EF80` in the friend's game; the host's scan found no BAR with the same fingerprint, nor a unique one with the same names. Source: two-PC log 2026-10-14. To verify: search the host's loaded files for it by name.
- **A Limit's motion file can sit at another address in each game:** (L43): Trinity's motions (252-256) were skipped on both sides by motion_mirror.cpp's address check ('that file isn't at the same place in our game'); Mulan's file was at `exe+0x1A42AD0` in both games. Source: two-PC run 2026-10-14. To verify: read Trinity's file address in both games.
- **How a Limit sends the allies away and brings them back:** (L46): Confirmed: the end mark comes about 1 s before `exe+0x2A24CC0` clears, see the leave/bring-back row. Lead: the start `exe+0x3D8B40` stores it at `exe+0x2A24CC0`, runs `exe+0x400990` on live (`exe+0x3BA720`) members 0 and 1 (`exe+0x3C3270`), unless +0x6C8 bit 22 or +0xDD4 != 0 (probably the partner). `exe+0x400990` finds it by `exe+0x3B5B20`, fades it, sets +0x9B8 bit 0x10 and appends its id (`exe+0x3E31C0`) to `exe+0x2AE9788` (count `exe+0x2AE9790`); `exe+0x400A30` does both (a caller `exe+0x3FCEC0`). The end `exe+0x3D8F40` calls `exe+0x400440` (not with +0x54 bit 4): each id rebuilt (`exe+0x3C2FC0`), faded in; also from `exe+0x3DE080`. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-14. To verify: the +0xDD4 rule.
- **Companions' emergency pick:** (L52): Confirmed: putting an actor into +0xF78 before the pick aims both heals at it, see F182. Lead: `exe+0x1B3A30(companion)` (called by the think `exe+0x1B53B0` right after it sets +0xF78) runs only while +0xF78 is set and +0x4A8 is 0; it runs the helper's (+0xDF8) action entries flagged 0x10 (then 0x20) in check mode and runs one that said yes at random: Cure `exe+0x1CC1A0`, or HP item `exe+0x1CC970` -> `exe+0x1B7D90(companion, item, target, 1)`. Both heals aim at +0xF78 first (`exe+0x3BEE60`). 'Needs help' `exe+0x3C1D30` = HP <= max / 4. Source: Ghidra, session 34 (TODO 1.58). To verify: the 'needs help' rule, on a bench.
- **Party member constructor and the two companion spots:** (L61): `exe+0x3C2DB0`, the constructor for object type 1 (Donald, Goofy, world allies), puts the actor in the first free entry of `exe+0x2A239B0` (u64[2], what GetFriend reads) and its index at actor+0xDD0; `exe+0x3C2870` / `exe+0x3C3470` clear it on removal. Player-class actors (Sora, our copy) never go there. Spawn factory `exe+0x3DF930`: object 0x236 -> the player's object (`exe+0x405190(0)`), then by type: 0 -> `exe+0x3A7CB0`, 1 -> `exe+0x3C2F00`. Resolver `exe+0x3DFEB0`: 0x236 -> `exe+0x2A24FA0` or `exe+0x405190`, 0x237/0x238 -> `exe+0x3C32E0(1/2)`, 0x319/0x31A -> `exe+0x3C3320(1/2)`, 0x3EE -> `exe+0x3C3320(3)`. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-12, session 28. To verify: `exe+0x2A239B0`, live.
- **Forced-solo rooms: the area script's Party command:** (L69): Confirmed: the party templates, the rewrite by `exe+0x3E31F0` and the HB Bailey fight, see their table rows. Lead: room scripts in `ard/us/<room>.ard` set the party with command 0x0F `Party` {u32}: 0 NO_FRIEND, 1 DEFAULT, 2 W_FRIEND, 3 W_FRIEND_IN, 4 W_FRIEND_FIX, 5 W_FRIEND_ONLY; a room scan counted 187 NO_FRIEND (90 btl, 97 evt). The interpreter `exe+0x3A24C0(ctx, command)` switches on the u16 opcode (4 Capacity -> f32 `exe+0x2A0F7DC`, 0x15 Mission, ...); 0x0F rewrites the current world's (u8 `exe+0x717008`) party entry, save+0x3534+4*world if the save starts at `exe+0x9A98B0`. Source: room files and Ghidra 2026-10-11 (TODO 5.5). To verify: the room scan and the interpreter's other cases.
- **Playing a motion from another file:** (L98): `exe+0x3C63E0(owner, bar, paxSlot, target, int motion, float blend, int* users)`: only caller `exe+0x3D93E0` (for `trap_limit_motion_start` 2/47); plays entry `motion` of the BAR with `exe+0x3C64A0`, then sets `target+0x2B8` = bar, `+0x180` = motion. `exe+0x3C6CC0(src+0x158, target, motion, float blend)` (`trap_obj_motion_capture` 1/29 and 12 others) does the same from the source's own file. `exe+0x3C64A0` stores paxSlot at `target+0x158+0x148`, the owner at `+0x158+0x158`, counts `*users` up; an actor's own motion (`exe+0x3C8CD0`) puts its own effects (`actor+0x80`) back and clears the owner. Source: Ghidra + disassembly 2026-10-12 (TODO 1.31). To verify: read those fields during a Limit.
- **The battle state and the Limit list:** (L132): Confirmed: see the rows `exe+0x3AB690`, `exe+0x2A11404`, the item and `limt` tables and the ability bits (bench 2026-10-25). Lead: the list builder `exe+0x3E7B30` greys each Limit unless `exe+0x3E75D0` (no Limit running, `exe+0x2A10508` = 0 or `exe+0x2A24EDC` = 2, not `exe+0x1650D0`, battle != 0, not `exe+0x3C1D10(stats)`), and lists one only if `exe+0x3E81A0` (player +0x6CB bit 0, world, partners present, ability bit); rooms whose battle byte +4 bit 0 is set force state 2 on load; state 2 hides the minimap (`exe+0x3ABD00`); `exe+0x3ABAB0` resets every battle global (likely on room load). Source: Ghidra 2026-10-24, session 42 (Track 2). To verify: the greying rules, on a bench.
- **How a Limit motion reaches its motion object:** (L254): `exe+0x3C65E0` finds the motion's entry in the Limit's BAR by name, decodes its +8 (a handle once the file is placed) into the inner BAR and calls `exe+0x3C8A40`, which decodes the first type-9 entry's +8 into the motion object, keeps it at state +0x18, calls its vtable +8 (frame count, to +0x40; the 1.119 abort) and keeps type 0x10's object at +0x20. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-10 (TODO 1.119). To verify: state +0x18 during a Limit motion.
- **A partner frozen in the Blizzard Lord's ice can't do their Limit:** (L261): In one bench run where Aladdin played the captured motion 252 from `B_AL120` (frozen in the ice block), Speedster looked greyed and 4 presses did nothing: no 'menu: command' line, the list dim. Not proven: no log of the game's own Limit state (`exe+0x3D88E0` result 5). Source: bench log (limit_friend) 2026-11-05. To verify: log `exe+0x3D88E0`'s result while the partner is frozen.
- **How a Limit counts its actors:** (L266): `exe+0x3D93E0` (2/47) calls `exe+0x3C63E0(0, bar, limit + 0x18, actor, motion, blend, limit + 0x50)`; `exe+0x3C65E0` keeps that pointer at actor+0x308 (motion state +0x1B0) and counts it up; each new motion set (`exe+0x3C8A40`) counts it down and clears it. Weapons likely count too. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-10 (TODO 1.180). To verify: read Limit +0x50 and actor+0x308 during a gauge-out.
- **What trap_limit_motion_start returns:** (L267): Confirmed: 2/47 returns the capture id 1/238 reads, see F487. Lead: the id comes from `exe+0x3C64A0` (`exe+0x74B980` counted down, also stored at target+0x184), and the trap handler `exe+0x432E20` stores it as the script's result. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-10 (TODO 1.180). To verify: read `exe+0x74B980` and target+0x184 around a 2/47 call.
- **Limits whose gauge-out wasn't traced:** (L268): in the decoded `limit/sp` scripts Aladdin, Riku, Goofy and the second Donald and Goofy Limits also keep 2/47's result; Donald, Jack, Mulan, Simba, Sparrow, Tron and Trinity drop it. Only Auron's gauge-out uses '258, land motion 5, wait'. Source: Bdxio decode 2026-11-10 (TODO 1.180). To verify: a gauge-out of each with the fixed hook.
- **When a Limit counts as usable for the preload:** (L299): `exe+0x3E7650` waits while `exe+0x39DCC0` is 1; a limt entry is usable in `exe+0x3E81A0` when the player's +0x6CB bit 0 is set, its world byte +0x2C is 0 or the current world, each partner passes `exe+0x3BA720`, and its ability item (+0x2A, type 0x13) is set on the first partner. Source: Ghidra, 2026-11-11. To verify: a bench with a partner's Limit ability off.
- **Who runs the bring-back after a Limit:** (L300): `exe+0x3D8F40` calls `exe+0x400440` when its object's +0x54 bit 0x10 is clear, else `exe+0x400830` (zeroes `exe+0x2AE9790`), then sets the current Limit's (`exe+0x2A24CC0`) +0x54 bit 2. That order conflicts with F509's delay, so another path may set the mark. Source: Ghidra, 2026-11-11. To verify: a hook log on `exe+0x400440`'s caller.
- **Who calls the party leave and the bring-back:** (L302): the leave `exe+0x400990` is called by the Limit start and `exe+0x400B20`; the bring-back `exe+0x400440` by the Limit end, the form ends, the summon end, `exe+0x414DD0` and `exe+0x4157D0`. Both are also referenced as data (`exe+0x2BBC9A8`, `exe+0x2BBC924`), maybe a script call table (not checked). Source: Ghidra xrefs, 2026-11-12. To verify: a hook log of their callers in story rooms.
- **The Limit preload's file asks:** (L307): `exe+0x3E80E0(row)` writes the row's path into `exe+0x2AE5698` ('limit/<lang>/<name>', language at `*(exe+0x8EC5D0)`); `exe+0x3E7650` asks it with `exe+0x39DF10(path, 5000, 7)` and the row's object (+0x24) with `exe+0x3E0460`, for at most 8 usable rows. The ability bit is read on row byte +1's character (Trinity: Sora's). Source: Ghidra 2026-11-12. To verify: read the name buffer on a bench.

### Drive Forms, bodies and Keyblades

- **The pause menu's Keyblade swap, and the copy's Keyblade:** (L5): `exe+0x3D59C0(?, character, slot, ?)` swaps a weapon: finds the actor among the player `exe+0x2A105D0` and the party (`exe+0x3C3270`) by character id `exe+0x3B5B20` (player first); ends the old weapon `exe+0x3EFF20`; lets go of its file group `exe+0x39D010`; asks for the new files `exe+0x3E0A20`, read `exe+0x39D460`; builds the weapon `exe+0x3EF750` at actor+0xD60 + 8 x slot; links weapon+0x158 to the owner's (`exe+0x3C8440`, `exe+0x3C86A0`). The copy's weapon 0 likely uses our group, so the drop takes its files; it is not rebuilt. The menu preview builds its own model (`exe+0x3E63D0`). Source: Ghidra 2026-10-22 (TODO 1.110). To verify: a pause-menu swap with the copy, on a bench.
- **Crash at `exe+0x3C859E` after a pause-menu Keyblade change:** (L6): With no friend connected, the motion step `exe+0x3C8520` crashed at `exe+0x3C859E` on the first frame after a Keyblade change: `call [rax+0x28]` through a motion object (controller +0x18) holding garbage. The controller was weapon +0x158 of the copy's weapon 0, so the copy's Keyblade still played files the swap had let go. Source: crash in the host's game, 2026-10-22. To verify: a pause-menu Keyblade swap with the copy, on a bench.
- **The friend's Wisdom Sora stuck after a room change in Wisdom:** (L7): Both times the friend's Sora froze ('T-pose when jumping') it had just entered a room in Wisdom and our stat-sheet fix had set the form link back. The copy, built after our Sora, writes the shared Sora sheet; our fix restores only +0x254, and the recalculation `exe+0x3D6850` for our Sora doesn't run. Not proven. Source: two-PC run over Steam 2026-10-22 (TODO 1.104). To verify: a Wisdom room change and jump, `KH2COOP_SORA_SHEET_FIX` on and off.
- **A form's second Keyblade:** (L10): The player constructor `exe+0x3A7A40` builds weapon 0 with `exe+0x3D7C70(actor, exe+0x3E0CC0(spawn record, 0), 0)` and, for forms 1-6 and 10 (actor+0xDE0), weapon 1 with `exe+0x3E0CC0(record, 1)` in slot 1. The copy, built as form 0, has only weapon 0, which is likely why Valor's second Keyblade is missing on it. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-21 (TODO 1.24). To verify: read the copy's weapon slot 1 in Valor on a bench.
- **How a Drive loads its files and builds the form body:** (L11): The form change `exe+0x40CE60(player, form)` makes a 0x38-byte task (+0 player, +8 form, +0xC object id from `exe+0x405190(form)`) whose body: drops the old form's files (`exe+0x39CCA0(5000)`, group 5000); asks for the new form's files (`exe+0x3E0660(objId, 5000, 4)`: model, motions, weapons `exe+0x3E0CC0`, linked objects); waits for them (`exe+0x39D460(task)`); builds the 0xE18-byte body from `exe+0x3DFEB0(objId)` with `exe+0x405030` (sets the form player `exe+0x2AE9A28`); ends the old body (class slot +0x38). Only one form's files are kept, so a form body can't be made in a game not in that form. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-21 (TODO 1.24). To verify: the group 5000 entries around a Drive.
- **How a body's Keyblades are built and ended:** (L14): `exe+0x3D7C70(owner, weaponObj, slot)` (weaponObj from `exe+0x3E0CC0`) allocates (`exe+0x152430`), builds `exe+0x3EF750` on `exe+0x3DFEB0(weaponObj)` at owner+0xD60 + 8 x slot, links weapon+0x158 (`exe+0x3C8440`, `exe+0x3C86A0`). Weapon +0xBF8 owner, +0xC08 slot, +0xC0C group; vtable +0x38 ends it via actor end `exe+0x3B4700`; if named in `*(exe+0x2AE5A40)`, motion file from `exe+0x39CC40`, controller linked to the owner's: owner motions restart it. In one bench run (Valor + Wisdom) the copy's Keyblade used Valor's `W_EX010_BTLF_R.mset`; re-putting the copy's body motion after the friend's Drive ended the friend's game (fixed in our DLL). Source: Ghidra 2026-10-21. To verify: a live weapon.
- **A Roxas look on Sora's moves: the model's bones:** (L21): Model files read with a BAR parser: the model is entry type 4, its header after 0x90 bytes, with a u16 bone count at +0x10 and a u32 bone offset at +0x14; bones are 0x40 bytes with the parent id at +4. Source: research session 39, 2026-10-21 (docs/Roxas_Model.md). To verify: check against OpenKH's model reader.
- **Changing a player's Keyblade at run time:** (L58): `exe+0x3D7C70(actor, weapon object, slot)` builds a weapon actor (ctor `exe+0x3EF750`) into `actor+0xD60 + 8 x slot`. `exe+0x3D59C0(?, character, slot, Keyblade item)` is the game's swap (one caller, likely the menu equip). For the copy (character 1) it would pick our own Sora, and its per-kind weapon file slot may replace our Sora's Keyblade. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-13 (TODO 3.3). To verify: swap the copy's Keyblade on a bench, check our Sora's.
- **Drive gauge bytes in Sora's stat slot:** (L96): In Sora's stat slot `exe+0x2A23598`, +0x1B0 = 0x64 (100, fill of the current bar?), +0x1B1 = 3 and +0x1B2 = 3 while the HUD showed 'MAX 3'; +0x1AF = 1 (the state byte `exe+0x3A8800` checks). Source: live read 2026-10-08, not confirmed by a change. To verify: change these bytes and watch the Drive gauge.
- **A player's Keyblade comes from the save, not the sheet:** (L127): `exe+0x3E0CC0(spawn record, slot)` reads the record of the spawn's char id (+0x4C) in `exe+0x9ABDA0` (+slot x 2), or for Sora/Roxas slot 1 the form record's item, then `exe+0x3EF8B0(kind, item)` gives the weapon object, so the copy gets our own Keyblade. File loads: `exe+0x3E0A20` -> `exe+0x3EF910(kind, slot)` -> `exe+0x39E000`. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-23, session 41 (TODO 3.3). To verify: the copy's Keyblade against the save's item, live.
- **An ended second Keyblade's delete reads our files:** (L137): Confirmed: the free time, see section 1 `exe+0x3B4E80`. An ended second Keyblade is deleted when the game gets to it: its detach (`exe+0x3DA250`) reads its motion object and its free (`exe+0x3B4E80`) its model, so freeing the files first crashed. Why one delete came late is unknown. Fixed in our DLL (files held until `exe+0x3B4E80` ran); not tried in play. Source: crash + bench 2026-10-25 (TODO 1.123). To verify: a form ending in play, no crash.
- **The pause menu's Keyblade swap frees the old weapon's files at once:** (L139): Confirmed: `exe+0x39D010(group)` frees the old weapon's files in the same call and `exe+0x3EF8B0(kind, item)` picks the new weapon object, see section 1 and F333. Lead: the free releases (`exe+0x3A6A10`), unlinks (`exe+0x39E2A0`) and frees each loaded entry of the group; `exe+0x3E0CC0` reads slot 1 of character 1 or 14 from the form record picked by entry byte +0x57. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-25 (TODO 1.110). To verify: that slot 1 pick, live.
- **The game's immediate Keyblade end:** (L140): Confirmed: see section 1 `exe+0x3EFF20`. `exe+0x3EFF20(weapon)` calls the weapon vtable (`exe+0x5C9408`) +0x80 `exe+0x3EF610` (hides: sets 0x700 in `+0x124`), +0x48 `exe+0x3EF520` (unlinks its controller from the owner's, clears the owner's `+0xD60 + 8 x slot`; `exe+0x3B45C0` ends it and marks it for the delete pass), then `exe+0x3B4E80` (frees its motion state and model instance now, sets `+0x9B8` bit 0x40 so the delete pass doesn't free them again). The menu swap runs it just before freeing the old files (L139). Source: Ghidra 2026-10-25 (TODO 1.110, 3.3). To verify: these inner steps live on a bench.
