- **The pause menu's Keyblade swap runs as a task:** (L142): `exe+0x36AF10(character, slot, item)` (not in Ghidra's function list) makes a task whose body `exe+0x36AF80` calls the swap `exe+0x3D59C0` (its only caller), so its file pass `exe+0x39D460(task)` waits inside the task. In one bench run, called from our spawn step outside the menu, it faulted at `exe+0x14F8BA` (null pointer): it needs the menu's task list. Source: Ghidra and a bench 2026-10-25 (TODO 1.110). To verify: the task body, live.
- **A form's body object is per world:** (L158): `exe+0x405190(form)` reads u16 at the current character's object row (`exe+0x3E2E40`) +0x16 + 2 x form, so each world has its own form bodies (live: Timeless River's Limit 0x961 and Final 0x670 against 0x95D and 0x58). form_look caches forms 1-10 at its first call, so in the costume worlds the copy keeps Sora's look (bench room sweep). Not built: per-world forms on the copy. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-29. To verify: form ids in each costume world.
- **Crashes building the copy's Keyblade past our file buffer:** (L178): Three crashes read a file pointer just past our 32 MB copy-file buffer: twice after a room load in `exe+0x2C6320` while `exe+0x3EF750` built the copy's Keyblade, once in MakeEffect `exe+0x2BD510` at its first effect. Each time its `.a.us` was new (not yet read); in good builds it was taken back (loaded). `copy_blade.cpp` waited for the `.mdlx` and `.mset` only. Source: two-PC run 2026-11-01. To verify: wait for the `.a.us` too, then repeat.
- **A form body starts with motion 53, a plain body with the Revert burst:** (L286): Confirmed: the copy makes motion 53's frame-0 effects at its look change, see F494. Lead: `exe+0x405630` plays motion 53 on a form body (each form `.mset`'s has 15 frame-0 type-1 triggers, ids 1-12, 100-102; `P_EX100.mset`'s none); a plain body plays motion 0 or 4, then the Revert loop (F475). Source: Ghidra, OpenKH, 2026-11-11. To verify: live.
- **Form bodies carry their own scripts; Wisdom's shots:** (L287): Confirmed: the shot's calls from the MAGF body's script, see F496; the KH1F body's script runs too, see F499. `P_EX100_MAGF.mdlx` (`wisd`) and `_KH1F.mdlx` (`limi`) hold a BDX; `P_EX100`, `_BTLF`, `_TRIF` and `_ULTF` don't. Wisdom's key-0x10 handler turns trigger ids 25-43 into the shot thread. Source: OpenKH files (BDX read by hand), 2026-11-11. To verify: the trigger ids that start a shot.

### File heap and loaded files

- **The file move's fields:** (L15): `exe+0x39E690(entry)` runs with +0x58 = the new place and +0x60 = the old one; the data stays at the old place until it copies it, and equal places mean no move. +0x44 bit 0 = done this layout, bit 1 = to free. Source: Ghidra and benches 2026-10-21 (TODO 1.24 second Keyblade). To verify: +0x58 and +0x60 of a moved file.
- **The file request's group:** (L16): In `exe+0x3E0660(objId, group, p3, p4)`, group -1 = the object's default, u32 `exe+0x5C8500[entry type byte +4]` (type 0 = 0x8FC = 2300, 1 = 0x960, 2 = 0x9C4, 3 = 0xA28, 4 = 0xA8C, ...), or -200 for a cutscene id (bit 30) whose type isn't 7, 22 or 24. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-21 (TODO 1.24 step 1). To verify: the group of a file asked with -1.
- **The file heap and how a request gets loaded:** (L17): `exe+0x3E0660` only queues files; `exe+0x39D460(task or 0)` loads them: with a task it waits (`exe+0x39E340`: while `exe+0x2A0D628` != 0, `exe+0x1DC340()` != 0 or a file of a group under 10000 is at state 1 or 2), lays out (`exe+0x39D590`), starts reads (`exe+0x39E690`). 30 MB heap (end pointer `exe+0x749740`; negative groups from the top, bottom `exe+0x29F33F0`). Entry +0x48 group, +0x70 next (`exe+0x4AD270`). A drop `exe+0x39CCA0(g)` adds 10000 to groups >= g under 10000: a cache until the layout needs room; `exe+0x39E000` revives a cached name. `exe+0x3E0160` = loaded once model and motions are in (weapons may lag). Source: Ghidra, benches 2026-10-21. To verify: read entries live.
- **The resource list:** (L18): The resource list's head is `exe+0x29F33C8`, entries linked by a handle at +0x70: +0x48 group / priority (5000 = the Drive Form's files; `exe+0x39CCA0(g)` re-ranks or frees a group), +0x68 object id, +0x44 bit 1 = to free. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-21. To verify: walk the list from its head on a bench.
- **Host abort() in our replay of the friend's Limit motion:** (L128): Confirmed: the cause, see F476 (a layout moved the file and the replay read the old copy). The host's game ended by abort(): a pure virtual call under the Limit motion play `exe+0x3C63E0`, called by our DLL's replay of the friend's Limit on the host's companion, after the Limit preload dropped group 5000; the dead object is L254. Fixed in our DLL (TODO 1.119). Source: two-PC log 2026-10-24, bench log 2026-11-10. To verify: no abort on two PCs.
- **A file asked for again joins the old entry, except in 0x8FD and 5002:** (L138): `exe+0x39E000(name, group, size)` first returns an entry of that name in that group; else the listed file of that name under group 10000, its group unchanged, unless the group asked is 0x8FD or 0x138A (5002): then a second entry is made. A dropped one (group >= 10000) takes the new group. So the copy's Keyblade files asked in a group of ours are shared with our Sora's when both carry the same Keyblade. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-26. To verify: live.
- **The file heap layout:** (L141): Confirmed: the place step, see section 1 `exe+0x39D590`. The file list is sorted by group (0 and up, then the negative ones). `exe+0x39D460` first runs `exe+0x39D590`, which only works out places: let-go entries (>= 10000) go to the list's end, then each entry gets the next place in its area (0x3F2: 0xE0000; 0x8FC-0x95F the player area, size `exe+0x29FB430`; 0x96A-0x973; 0x974-0x97D; 5000-5099 Drive, 0x300000; 5100-5199 0xC0000 when set; others general), negative groups from the heap's end down; old place at +0x60. Then `exe+0x39E690` moves or reads each entry, so a file moves when an earlier file of its area changes. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-25 (session 44). To verify: the areas' sizes.
- **A file is found by name and exact group:** (L143): `exe+0x39CC40(name, group)` returns the listed file with that name and exactly that group (-1 = any); with none, strstr on address 4 crashes (`exe+0x39CC89`). In one bench run the friend's Valor Drive crashed there after the bench wrote its record's Keyblade item 0x29 without the menu, so W_EX010 was never loaded in group 2301; with the change made in the menu, no crash. Source: Ghidra and crash 2026-10-25. To verify: which group it asks.
- **A motion-step crash when our Drive frees an earlier form's files:** (L145): In one bench run, with the copy in Wisdom, the game crashed at `exe+0x1A86C5` reading 0x304 (a null base) as copy_blade ended before a layout that may free `W_EX010_MAGF.mset` (group 15001, the game's own earlier Wisdom files the copy's look had joined) and form_look put the look on again from the moved files. Which actor's motion read it and why: not known. Source: bench log 2026-10-26 (TODO 1.127). To verify: which actor's motion reads it.
- **The draw packets are a PS2-style DMA chain; a crash there:** (L146): `exe+0x1ADED0` walks a chain of 16-byte commands: tag id bits 28-30 (1 cnt, 2 next, 3/4 ref, 5 call, 6 ret, as PS2 DMA tags; next and call decode a handle with `exe+0x4AD270`), opcode bits 16-25 picks a handler. Opcode 0x328 (`exe+0x1BA750`) decodes the handle at command +8 as a texture and sets the texture registers. In one two-PC run the friend's game crashed there (`exe+0x1BA7A3`, the handle decoded to unmapped memory) just after the copy's look went on again from moved files. Which draw packet still held the old handle: not known. Source: Ghidra and two-PC log 2026-10-26 (session 45; TODO 1.127). To verify: which packet holds the old handle.
- **Where the place step puts each group, and what can move a file:** (L147): `exe+0x39DD70` inserts after the files already in its group (F348). `exe+0x39D590` places in list order: negative groups from `*(exe+0x749740)` down (bottom at `exe+0x29F33F0`); every other area starts where the files before it in group order end and reserves its size (0x3F2 `exe+0x29FB42C`, 0x8FC-0x95F +0x430, 0x96A-0x973 +0x434, 0x974-0x97D +0x438, 5000-5099 +0x43C, 5100-5199 +0x440 when above 0, general `exe+0x29FB428`). Then `exe+0x39D460` frees, not moves, every group 0+ entry whose new place ends above that bottom (`exe+0x39E2A0`, data by `exe+0x3A6A10(data, 1)`), leaving pointers dangling. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-26, corrected 2026-10-29. To verify: a game file lost this way.
- **The drop lets go of negative groups too:** (L148): `exe+0x39CCA0(from)` lets go of every group >= from (from >= 0) or <= from (from < 0). A group under 10000 becomes 29000 (one of the two at `exe+0x749758`), 15400 + i (the i-th of the 13 at `exe+0x749760`), 30000 if negative, else group + 10000; one already let go ages by +50. Only from == 5000 frees the group-30000 entries at once (`exe+0x39E2A0`). Source: Ghidra 2026-10-26 (session 45). To verify: which value the room's unload passes, live.
- **A set's file lookup wants the file in the group it asked for:** (L149): `exe+0x39CC40(name, group)` returns the entry's place (+0x58) in exactly that group (-1 any); if none, it reads address 4. The set fill `exe+0x3E6120` uses the set's group for model and texture (-1 for the third). A weapon's set group (`exe+0x3D7C70`) is its owner's group + slot + 1, or 0x8FD for a player's slot 0 in some state. F350 and F352 were this lookup in our group + 2. Source: Ghidra and bench 2026-10-26. To verify: a weapon's set group.
- **The heap's end is a stack of blocks taken from the top:** (L150): `exe+0x39D300(size)` lowers `*(exe+0x749740)` by size (rounded up to 0x80), pushes the size at `exe+0x29FB400[--*(u32*)exe+0x749750]`, marks every file under 10000 whose data reaches above the new end as unloaded, frees the let-go ones there and clears the block at once; `exe+0x39D400(block)` raises the end by the size on top of that stack (by index, not address). So negative-group files at the end are wiped the moment a block is taken, before any layout. Takers include the room load's 0x80000 and the cutscene's 2 MB pool (L154). Source: Ghidra 2026-10-26 (session 45). To verify: a negative-group file after a block is taken.
- **No pin flag at +0x44:** (L151): The place pass `exe+0x39D590` moves let-go entries (>= 10000) to the list's end, re-inserts every entry, sets +0x44 = 0 on all and places each. +0x44 bit 0 = placed in this pass (set by `exe+0x39E690`), bit 1 = a let-go entry with no data; both last one pass. `exe+0x39E690` also re-points a moved file's resource object (+0x658, +0x10, the +0x670 chain's +0x80). Source: Ghidra 2026-10-26 (session 45). To verify: +0x44 after a layout.
- **A cutscene's work block size comes from its script, up to 4 MB:** (L153): The cutscene start `exe+0x2CC4B0(script, ...)` sets the size to (float)script+4 + a constant from `exe+0x5B8F7C`, then if not 0 adds 0x40000 and caps it at 0x400000; 0 takes no block. The setup task `exe+0x2CC180` takes the block and the task's end `exe+0x2C85B0` gives it back. So any event mid-room (summon entries, boss scenes L65) can lower the heap's end by up to 4 MB. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-26. To verify: the heap's end during a summon.
- **The cutscene work block's life:** (L154): `exe+0x2CCAB0` keeps the block at `exe+0xB64F88`. One give-back: the task's end via `exe+0x2CE470` to `exe+0x39D400`, which first tears down every resource object (list `exe+0x8AEF30`) overlapping it (`exe+0x12EF30`; a 'BAR' block: `exe+0x3A6A10(block, 1)`), so a buffer of ours needs the same teardown. At the 4 MB cap an event also takes a 2 MB pool (`exe+0x2CF090` / `exe+0x2CF220`, returning to `exe+0x2CF0E9` / `exe+0x2CF28A`; its own sub-allocator, not the effect pool's `exe+0x4AF380`). `exe+0x2CC4B0` stores a script only when the runner's one slot is free, so no second event takes a block while one is out. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-26. To verify: the teardown at a summon's give-back.
- **Who runs the Limit preload:** (L155): `exe+0x3D8830` runs `exe+0x3D9130` while a Limit runs (`*(exe+0x2A24CC0)` not 0; not a Drive); otherwise, when the player actor's class check passes and `exe+0x3A57F0` returns 0, `exe+0x3E7650`: for each usable `limt` entry whose files aren't all in (`exe+0x3E0160`), it drops from 5000, asks each Limit's files in 5000 (`exe+0x3E0460`) and calls `exe+0x39D460(0)`. So it reloads whenever a usable Limit's files are out: after a Revert (F357), a summon's end. `exe+0x39D460(task)` waits (`exe+0x39E340()`) before the place step and `exe+0x39E340(task)` after the moves; with 0 it returns with every file moved. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-27, corrected in session 46. To verify: the reload after a summon.
- **MOOSE, a DLL that enlarges KH2's file buffers:** (L160): KH2 MOOSE sets the size of 'the cache buffer' (our 30 MB file region) and of 'preset blocks' such as `player` (L141); its page says all buffers together must stay under 2 GiB. Installs as dbghelp.dll or dinput8.dll (Panacea is our dbghelp). Risk: a region moved under our copy-files buffer would make the top take (L150) unload our files. Source: another project's page, read 2026-10-29; not tried on this build. To verify: log the region with MOOSE.

### Effects, particles and lights

- **The Drive's glow stays on the copy:** (L12): Confirmed: the Drive's glow stayed on the copy (fixed in our DLL), see F313. In bench screenshots, in the game not in that form, the copy in a form was a white glowing shape with sparkles; the real form body in its own game has no glow. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-21 and bench screenshots, for TODO 1.24. To verify: the sparkles on the copy in a form, after the F313 fix.
- **The effect hard stop:** (L13): `exe+0x2BE8A0(instance)` is the hard stop: it sets bits 0x10001 in instance +0xC, deletes the particle system at +0x30 (`exe+0x2C1F50`) and ends its sound. In one bench run the copy's Drive shine vanished at once with it. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-21 (TODO 1.24). To verify: read instance +0xC and +0x30 after the call.
- **The effect system's mode:** (L37): `exe+0xB63628` is the effect system's mode: 1 when the current scene is the field one (`exe+0x150820`), 2 for the other (`exe+0x151B00`); set by `exe+0x2C4330` / `exe+0x2C4540`. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-16, session 36, from the friend's crash in the two-PC run 2026-10-15. To verify: read `exe+0xB63628` in the field and in the other scene.
- **Friend crash in Data Roxas: the effect pool full, at `exe+0x4D3A84`:** (L54): In Data Roxas the friend's game faulted twice at a new site `exe+0x4D3A84`, writing address 0x4 (rbx 0), with the effect pool full; the stack runs through our effect step hook. Source: crash in the two-PC run 2026-10-13 (session 33) and Ghidra. To verify: read the effect pool's use when the fault comes.
- **Where the effect pool is made, and its size:** (L55): Confirmed: the pool setup `exe+0x4AF380` and a buffer elsewhere, see the table. Lead: `exe+0x2C4330(heap, size1, size2)` -> `exe+0x2C2480` makes the pool with `exe+0x4AF380` at `exe+0xB60808` (+0 rover, +8 start, +0x10 end = start + size - 0x10, +0x18 bytes in use) and sets `exe+0x2B0EC08` = `exe+0xB60808`. Callers pass the heap `*(exe+0x9BA920)` with size1 0x80000, or u32s from `exe+0x2A0F7D4`: it runs again on loads. Handle = `0x80000000` OR slot << 25 OR (address AND 0x1FFFFFF); the 64-slot base table `exe+0x2B0D720` only grows. The allocator `exe+0x4AF240` has no fixed pool size. Not checked: when the game frees its buffers. Source: Ghidra, session 33. To verify: a bigger pool.
- **Effect instances and Limit objects:** (L97): `exe+0x2C4050(inst)` zeroes a 0x30-byte PAX instance; `exe+0x2C6320(inst, pax, 0)` fills it (+0 pax, +8 pax + 0x10) and links it at the end of a game-wide list (head `exe+0xB63608`, next as a handle at +0x28). The Limit object (0x128 bytes from `exe+0x152430`) has its instance at +0x18, filled by `exe+0x3D96D0` from the Limit's file. An attack (`exe+0x3CF830`) keeps its effect instance as a handle at +0x28: its owner's +0x80, the running Limit's (attack data +0x12 bit 1) or that of the actor at owner +0x2B0 (bit 3). `exe+0x4AD240` encodes any pointer, adding its 32 MB block to the 64-entry table `exe+0x2B0D720`. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-12, session 29. To verify: the list during a Limit.
- **Effect instance fields:** (L102): Confirmed: see F217.
- **How a killed effect is finished, and an actor's effect records:** (L120): Confirmed: an effect that runs out of particles (the step `exe+0x2C28E0` returns 0) gets 0x10000 without bit 0 and is never destroyed, see F268. Lead: the kill `exe+0x2BE8A0` (0/51) sets 0x10001 and frees the part; `exe+0x2BE8F0` (loop end) sets bit 0. The sweep `exe+0x2BD730` destroys a killed effect when bit 20 is set, and sets bit 20 when bit 0 is set. Holders: list head `exe+0xB63608`, next holder's handle +0x28, newest record +0x18, oldest +0x20; an actor's holder is actor+0x80. `exe+0x2C6320(holder, pax, flag)` fills one (L283); `exe+0x2C6490(holder)` kills every record's effect and frees the records. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-15, session 36. To verify: `exe+0x2C6490`, live.
- **trap_effect_set_pos (0/38) writes into the effect's part:** (L192): Table 0 is `*(exe+0x753490)` = `exe+0x752E00` (16-byte entries). Setters 0/38 `exe+0x41DF80`, 0/39 `exe+0x41E000`, 0/40 `exe+0x41E080`, 0/41 `exe+0x41E100` and 0/67 `exe+0x41E520` zero NaNs in the vector, then copy 16 bytes with `exe+0x1A8E60` into the part `*(effect+0x30)`, so F369's crash at `exe+0x1A8E67` is a null part. Part: +0 place (`exe+0x2BE970`), +0x10 rotation (`exe+0x2BE1B0`; 0/41 turns its direction into it with `exe+0x3B7710`), +0x20 scale (`exe+0x2BEA80`), +0x30 color (`exe+0x2BE1E0`). Source: Ghidra 2026-11-06 and 2026-11-12. To verify: a null part when 0/38 runs, on a bench.
- **The hit effect maker with any holder:** (L256): `exe+0x3F6410` reads only the holder (+0 PAX, +8 0x50-byte entries, +0x10 DPX), writes records (+0x18 / +0x20) and effect +0x10 = holder, without checking holder +0 (the game checks first; its hits pass owner 0). a, b, c = effect +0xC flags, +8 kind, +0x18: (1, 1, 10000) for hits, (1, 1, 0) for triggers; b = 4: no part, a crash. In mode 2 (`exe+0xB63628`) owner 0 is read unchecked. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-10 (TODO 1.31). To verify: our own holder.
- **A motion's effect triggers and its holder:** (L257): `exe+0x3C65E0` keeps the holder at state +0x148 until the next ordinary motion start puts back actor +0x80. Trigger type 1 (`exe+0x40DD70`) makes the effect when holder +0 isn't 0, owner the actor; the Limit's script then gets key 0x18 only if the holder is the running Limit's +0x18, else the actor's script gets key 0x10. The game fills free-standing holders itself (`exe+0x2D9340`). Source: Ghidra 2026-11-10. To verify: state +0x148, live.
- **The game's own Limit holder's life:** (L258): `exe+0x3D96D0` fills limit +0x18 with the Limit BAR's type-0x12 entry only for Limits where `exe+0x3E82B0(kind)` is false (the others' effects are the Limit actor's +0x80). The end mark changes nothing; about 1 s later, once +0x18 has no records, the game runs `exe+0x2C6490` and `exe+0x2C5730`, releases the BAR (`exe+0x39DA10`), sets `exe+0x2A24CC0` = 0 and frees the Limit. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-10. To verify: `exe+0x2A24CC0` at a Limit's end.
- **Holder list rules:** (L259): `exe+0x2C6320` links at the tail even if already linked, so a refill mid-list cuts the rest off: `exe+0x2C40C0` (in-list check, then `exe+0x2C5730`), then `exe+0x2C4050`, then fill. `exe+0x2C6490` and `exe+0x2C5730` kill each record's effect at once (`exe+0x2BE8A0` reads file data, inferred), so a holder is ended before its file moves. Handle table `exe+0x2B0D720` (64 slots) gives slot 0 when full. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-10. To verify: a refill.
- **The Limit script's effects:** (L260): `trap_limit_effect_start` (2/57), `_pos` (2/58) and `_bind` (2/59) are the only callers of `exe+0x3D9B60(limit, id, a, owner)` = `exe+0x2C6B30(limit+0x18, id, a, 1, 30000, owner)`: it makes the effect with `exe+0x2BD510` and pushes a 0x78-byte record at holder +0x18. `trap_effect_kill` (0/51) = `exe+0x2BE8A0`. Trap tables at `exe+0x753490`, 16 bytes an entry. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-10 (TODO 1.31). To verify: the record layout and the flags 0x21 call `exe+0x3C8000` on a bench.
- **An actor's effect holder is inline, filled from its `.a.us`:** (L283): Confirmed: `actor+0x80` is the actor's own holder, refilled in place by our DLL, see F493. Lead: the base init `exe+0x3B3200` zeroes it (`exe+0x2C4050`) and `exe+0x3DA940` fills it with `exe+0x2C6320(actor+0x80, pax, flag)` from the first type-0x12 entry of `actor+0x928`'s `.a.us` (`exe+0x3A6420`); flag 1 only for `B_EX170`. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-11. To verify: live.
- **Type-1 motion triggers find their effect by id:** (L284): Confirmed: see F493.
- **A file move doesn't fix effect holders:** (L285): `exe+0x39E690` fixes the PC port's file object found by name (`exe+0x12DF90`), and `exe+0x3A6120` / `exe+0x2C4530` re-encode the BAR's and the PAX's own handles. Nothing walks the holder list `exe+0xB63608`, so holder +0, record +0x48 and effect +0x20 keep the old place. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-11. To verify: a holder's +0 after a layout moved its `.a.us`.
- **An effect start with no owner:** (L288): Confirmed: see F496.
- **The delete and a motion start clear a motion's effect list:** (L289): The delete handler `exe+0x3DA6C0` (class +0x30 of vtable `exe+0x5C6CC0`) runs `exe+0x3C7300(motion state)`, then `exe+0x2C6490` and `exe+0x2C5730` on `actor+0x80` if its +0 isn't 0. The motion start `exe+0x3C8A40` calls `exe+0x3C7300` first: it flags and empties state +0x1F8. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-11. To verify: the copy's class uses this handler.
- **The copy's own landed hits read its holder:** (L290): Confirmed: see F493.
- **A room's exit ends every effect holder before its drop:** (L291): On the three room-exit paths (`exe+0x152A90`, `exe+0x1524E0`, the load task `exe+0x152680`), `exe+0x2C4240` runs before the drop `exe+0x39CCA0(0)`: it zeroes the holder list `exe+0xB63608` / `exe+0xB63610` and frees every effect's particle system (`exe+0x2BD980`), so no later kill reads a file. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-11. To verify: the holder list is empty at the room's drop.
- **The copy's class and its cached effects:** (L292): The copy is a `SORA` actor (vtable `exe+0x5CB368`, built by `exe+0x405030`, 0xE18 bytes; the player factory `exe+0x3A7CB0` makes none over 0x1000). Its effect slots +0xD38 and +0xE08 come from the global holder `exe+0x2AE7508`, +0xDE8 / +0xDF8 from `exe+0x162530`'s; none caches one of its +0x80's. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-11. To verify: the bench's scan before each holder end.
- **An effect on a missing Keyblade crashes:** (L298): Confirmed: the empty-slot crash and the slot-1 path, see F507. In `exe+0x3B50F0`, odd codes 0x4003-0x4007 use slot 0 and even ones slot 1; the weapon path is taken only with `actor+0x6C8` bit 2, else the actor's own +0xAB8 list; `exe+0x3B5D40` reads the weapon's +0xAB8; `exe+0x2C4280` re-places live effects each frame. Source: Ghidra, 2026-11-11. To verify: a guard line for slot 0.
- **The holder task re-places some records' effects every frame:** (L303): Confirmed: the bound case (`(+0x70 & 0x11) == 1`, flags 0x41), see F521. `exe+0x2C58F0(record)`, from the holder task `exe+0x2C4280` (`exe+0x2C5BE0` walks a whole holder), rewrites the effect's part each frame: for `record+0x70` bit 1 from the camera (`exe+0x2C4DA0`), for the bound case from the attached matrix (`exe+0x2C49D0`). `exe+0x2C6B30` sets bit 0 from the PAX entry's +0x10 bit 0 and bit 1 from its flag 0x20000. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-12 (TODO 1.189). To verify: poll an effect whose entry has flag 0x20000 each frame on a bench.

### World sync and one brain

- **A one-brain enemy's brain must stay off while it is removed:** (L45): The friend's `F_EH110` (TWTNW 0x12/0x1C, the RC object of the chase before Xemnas) was fade-removed after the host's was deleted; its brain `exe+0x3B4460` ran again that frame, its script started 're_f_eh110-00', and the game crashed at `exe+0x3E1271` reading through `0xEFACCAFEEFACCAFE` (freed memory, filled with 0xEFACCAFE). Source: two-PC crash 2026-10-14, cause from the logs, fix not tried. To verify: redo the chase with the brain kept off.
- **Friend crash at `exe+0x3C795C`: a motion the set lacks:** (L87): Script call `exe+0x42F2D0` asks `exe+0x3C7950(actor+0x158, motion id)` for a motion's data; `exe+0x3C7A00` looks it up (`exe+0x3C7C50`) and returns 0 when the set lacks it, and `exe+0x3C7950` then reads [0] and crashes. So a script asking for a motion its actor's set lacks crashes. This crash's cause was in our code (TODO 1.41). Source: bench crash 2026-10-08. To verify: ask for a missing motion on a bench.
- **The friend aborted making a host event's props after a reload:** (L179): In one two-PC run (BC 05/0B, the host's 'Ask for Help') world sync's make path (factory `exe+0x3DF930`) hit a pure virtual call in the actor setup `exe+0x3DA940` for objects 0x193 `H_BB050_TSURU` and 0x197 `F_BB550`: objentry type 2, made by the host's event with no serial, sent as NPCs since 1.163. The fix in our DLL: NPCs only with a serial (`IsNpc`). Source: two-PC run 2026-11-01. To verify: the same event after a reload on two PCs.

### Boss fights

- **The friend's copy of the shared desperation move never ends:** (L1): In one two-PC run the shared desperation move started together but never ended on the friend: its Xemnas's HP stayed 98 and its Sora stayed held. On the host the last hit (98 -> 1) was followed by `trap_obj_hook_stop`. Our DLL blocks damage on every bound enemy on the friend, also one on its own brain, so that HP can't move; likely the script waits for that drop. Source: two-PC log 2026-10-22. To verify: let that HP drop and watch for hook_stop.
- **Data Xemnas's shared move started 89 s late on the friend:** (L4): In one two-PC run the friend's own-brain Data Final Xemnas started act `btl_super_hard` with the host's, but its '-60' came 89 s late, as the host's move ended. Meanwhile the friend's motion mirror used that Xemnas as the source of the host's captures on the copy. Likely it was busy as the copy's captor, so its own capture of our Sora waited; not proven. Source: two-PC log 2026-10-22. To verify: log both captures' start and end.
- **The die / card and capture-form script calls:** (L27): Call tables at `exe+0x753490`. 1/242 `trap_player_capture_form` = `exe+0x42FE80`: player = the u64 at `exe+0x2AE9A28` (not `exe+0x2A105D0`); queues a 0x38-byte task (`exe+0x152430`) for form 12 with the object id from args[0] (a body swap); 1/243 `_end` = `exe+0x42FEA0`: back to form save+0x3524. 6/27 `trap_player_dice` = `exe+0x53DC30`: `exe+0x3B4DA0(*(exe+0x2A105D0), 0x2B)`; 6/29 `trap_player_card` = `exe+0x53DC50`: the same with 0x2E; their specs 6/28 `exe+0x53DC70` (4 floats), 6/30 `exe+0x53DC90` (7 floats). The form change `exe+0x40CE60` checks and stores the u8 at `exe+0x9ACDD4` (save+0x3524). Source: exe disassembly 2026-10-17 (TODO 1.90; fits F221). To verify: log the calls there.
- **The controller reads in Final Xemnas's Riku part:** (L49): 0/81 `trap_pad_is_trigger(n)` = `exe+0x41DAE0` tests bit n of the u64 at `exe+0xBF31A0 + pad*0x68 + 8` for pad 0 (`exe+0x39B780(0)`): the first pad, whoever the script is about. 1/113 `trap_player_get_movement(actor)` = `exe+0x42FE30` returns the vector at `*(actor+0xDD0)+0xB50`, presumably the stick. Both pass our trap thunk, so their answers could be replaced; not tried. Source: Ghidra, session 35. To verify: replace one on a bench.
- **Twin Lords: a short no-damage moment at HP 344:** (L56): In some bench runs the host applied the friend's claims on one Lord at HP 344 with no HP change for a moment; in another run the same Lord passed 344 without stopping. Starting HP 422-432, so 344 is about 80 %: likely a phase point of the fight in the game itself. Source: bench log, session 32 (`test_census.py twins`). To verify: find that HP check in Ghidra; not checked.
- **When Final Xemnas's grab (the Riku takeover) comes:** (L62): Confirmed: the bench facts on the grab, see F170. Lead: both crashes at the grab came with `B_EX170_LAST` (1500 max HP) at 230 HP and 187 HP, the second with Sora in motion 261 'other set' just before. The rule for when it comes is still unknown. Source: two-PC logs 2026-10-11/12, session 28. To verify: log Xemnas's HP at each grab on a bench.
- **Final Xemnas grab crash: script command on a missing actor:** (L66): Confirmed: Final Xemnas's grab crashes the game when Riku is missing, see the table row of that name. Lead: the fault at `exe+0x3D76DF` (reading `[0 + 0xD48]` in `exe+0x3D76D0(actor, obj)`) comes from script command `exe+0x42CA20`, whose handle decodes (`exe+0x4AD270`) to a 0 actor; obj from `exe+0x3E1970(.., {id, 0x52444140}, n)`. Source: crash + Ghidra, two-PC runs 2026-10-12. To verify: read that actor in memory at the grab.
- **Final Xemnas's cylinder scripts:** (L183): `F_EH080` / `F_EH070` share a 24-byte block `trap_teamwork_alloc(51, 24)`: +4 which cylinder Sora is on, +8 / +12 'this side fell' (`F_EH080` writes +8), +16 an RC sequence running. A cylinder whose own mission gauge (0 or 1) reaches 0 starts 'fall_soon'; leaving it then marks its side fallen (motion 191). The RC handler after `-00`: both fallen -> `-50`; only the other fallen -> motions 256 and 257 here, then 255 from the other cylinder's set only if it still exists, +4 not flipped (Sora lands back on the same cylinder); else 253/254/255 and +4 flips. Both `.mset` files hold 256/257. Source: bdx decode 2026-11-02. To verify: never seen live; reach the one-side-fallen RC on a bench.

### Rooms, loading and missions

- **RequestTransition internals:** (L64): With byte `exe+0x9BA8D1` 0, `exe+0x152990` makes a load task kept in `exe+0x9BA928`; with it set it only redirects the packet to `exe+0x717120`. The task sets in-field `exe+0x9BA8D0` = 0, tears the room down, commits the location (`exe+0x152BE0`), schedules LoadRoom and clears `exe+0x9BA928`. Reading: two requests before the task runs = two teardowns = the `exe+0x39DA55` crash. Source: Ghidra session 28. To verify: two requests in one frame.
- **Mission end commands:** (L81): Confirmed: the command call at `exe+0x41C4FA` and both endings, seen live in the Pete fight, see F107. Mission scripts run in `exe+0x41B400`: 16-bit words, opcode = word & 0xF, 10 = call command, trap = word >> 6, index = next word; entry `table[trap] + 16 x index` = {fn, u32 arg count / flags, 0x40000000 = returns a value}. Tables `exe+0x753490`; trap 4 `exe+0x757250` = mission commands: #3 `exe+0x4334C0` Mission Complete, #16 `exe+0x4334D0` likely Mission Failed, #22 `exe+0x4334F0` end flags + trigger 0xF. Mission object `exe+0x2A0FF68`, task `exe+0x3F4860`. Source: Ghidra, exe bytes, `msn/us/*.bar` scan, 2026-10-10. To verify: log the command number.
- **TT 02/08: the after-fight scene starts by time or HP:** (L92): In Twilight Town 02/08, event 707 starts after about 90 s of the Dusk fight or when a Sora drops to 30% HP; one bench run fits (707 started at once with the host's Sora low). Source: the user's account of the game rule, 2026-10-10. To verify: let the fight run 90 s at full HP on a bench.
- **Neoshadow and a test room for it:** (L110): Neoshadow = `M_EX420` (their objectId 120; Shadow `M_EX020` = 302, Soldier `M_EX010` = 301 there). In their run, Twilight Town `0x02/0x1F` battle program 5 spawned 2 Neoshadows and 2 Crimson Jazz. Source: another project (Volpestyle `docs/ENEMY_PARITY.md`, 'T1 batch 4'), read 2026-10-12 (TODO 1.2 / 1.55). To verify: load that room and program on a bench and list the enemies.
- **Borough battle programs (hb09.ard):** (L112): Confirmed: program 1 (Soldier `M_EX010` 301, Shadow `M_EX020` 302, the first visit), see rows 'Spawn group table' and `actor+0x9F0`. Btl programs: 1 = `b_10` + `b_00`; 2 = `b_10` + `b_01` (adds `M_EX530` obj 5); 3 = `b_11` + `b_02` (adds `M_EX710` obj 122); 4 = `b_11` + `b_03` (5 Neoshadows `M_EX420` obj 120, 7 `M_EX530`, 2 `M_EX710`); 0x0A = `b_12` + `b_20` (`M_EX610` obj 1114); 0x0B = `b_10` + `b_21` (7 Neoshadows); 0x33 / 0x3A / 0x48 = mission fights; 0x63 / 0x64 = the skateboard mission. Which map program the second visit uses with btl 4 is not known. Source: OpenKH tools on `ard/us/hb09.ard`, 2026-10-13. To verify: `tools/test_census.py neo` (map 1, btl 4).
- **The mission object and its helpers:** (L117): The mission is `M = *(exe+0x2A0FF68)`: `exe+0x3A3970` returns it, `exe+0x3A3B50` = M != 0, `exe+0x3A39F0` = setters allowed (M set and not `M+4` bit 2). `exe+0xABB850` is the mission clock (+1 a frame while `exe+0xABB854` is 0). Source: Ghidra 2026-10-15 (session 36, for mission_sync.cpp). To verify: read them on a bench during a mission fight.
- **Betwixt and Between by warp with programs 160 hangs:** (L131): In two bench runs (two copies, then one game alone), entered by warp with programs 160/160/160, Betwixt and Between hung after cutscene 011: the game asked for 02/28 programs 161/161/161 with every actor gone and the load never started (in-field stayed 1). So not the link; likely the warp skips earlier story state. With event program 0 the mission's end left the camera held. Source: bench logs 2026-10-24. To verify: reach it through the story.
- **Agrabah 07/08 by warp crashes at `exe+0x3F5E1C`:** (L262): In the room sweep, loading Agrabah 07/08 by the debug warp with the game's programs (0/0/0 here) crashed both games at `exe+0x3F5E1C` (writing 0x0, rbx 0) in the room load, right after the room's file drop. Not known whether the room needs other programs (warp-only) or our spawn hook is involved. Source: room sweep bench 2026-10-29. To verify: reach 07/08 by walking in.
- **Armored Xemnas II's room by warp crashes the host at `exe+0x3DA524`:** (L263): In the room sweep, loading 12/17 by the debug warp with programs 0/0/0 (not his fight's 73) crashed the host at `exe+0x3DA524`, reading the attach field 0x6A0 of a null actor: `B_EX180` calls `trap_obj_pos` at pc 10031 on an actor that isn't there, through our trap pass-through. Seen twice; not in the `armored2` bench with program 73, so likely warp-only. Source: room sweep bench 2026-10-29. To verify: walk into the fight.
- **Port Royal 09/0A by warp crashes at `exe+0x3A59AA`:** (L264): In the room sweep, loading Port Royal 09/0A by the debug warp with the game's programs crashed both games at `exe+0x3A59AA` (reading 0x32 of a null pointer) in the room load, with no frame of ours on the stack, like 07/08. Likely warp-only. Source: room sweep bench 2026-10-29. To verify: reach 09/0A by walking in.

Likely event-only rooms; 04/1D, 04/20, 04/24, 04/26 and 04/28 after them could not be measured. Source: room sweep bench 2026-10-29. To verify: reach them through the story.

Source: Ghidra 2026-11-12, OpenKH catalog. To verify: log both from a scene-end request to the load start.

### Menus, HUD and controller input

- **Pause menu (menu 8): item lists, text and custom items:** (L67): Confirmed: our own list, callback, message hook, line text and closing, see the row 'Pause menu with our own item list'. `exe+0x2E4C20(src, def)` opens menu 8 (Start: when `exe+0xABB878` = 0, `exe+0x9BA8D0` != 0; also game over `exe+0x3FCEC0`). Lists at `*(exe+0x2A11498)`, 0x40 bytes: +0 u16 id, +4 up to 4 {u16 kind, u16 msg id}, +0x18 u32 text pointer. Kind 5/0x13 Skip Scene `exe+0x2CFCD0`, 6 close `exe+0x2EB790`, any unhandled kind `def+0x28(kind, task)`. Fill `exe+0x31F1F0`, text lookup `exe+0x3AD9A0(msgId)`, +0x18 sprite `*(exe+0xBEF518)+0x220`. 32-bit pointers: `exe+0x4AD270`, 64 bases at `exe+0x2B0D720`. Source: Ghidra + OpenKH files 2026-10-11 (TODO 6.8). To verify: other lists.
- **Party HUD classes:** (L82): Confirmed: the list, set/clear, room-change clear, bit 4 and the update hook, see the table row `exe+0xABCFE8`. Lead: the HUD classes are `dk::GAUGE_PLAYER` (vtable `exe+0x5B2100`), `dk::GAUGE_FRIEND` (`exe+0x5B2158`, ctor `exe+0x16B5A0`, size 0x1710), `dk::FACE`, `dk::GAUGE_ENEMY`. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-09 (TODO 3.11). To verify: break in `exe+0x16B5A0` at a room start.
- **Party widget events and KO state:** (L85): `dk::GAUGE_FRIEND` vtable `exe+0x5B2158`: +0x08 update `exe+0x182730`, +0x28 refresh (event bit 0), +0x30 / +0x38 / +0x40 hit reactions (bits 1/2/3, raised by the hit handlers `exe+0x3D60C0` / `exe+0x3D5370`), +0x48 HP changed (bit 4). No separate KO event: the dispatcher `exe+0x17EE60` at the start of each update sets `gauge+0x20` = 2 (KO) when `actor+0x9B8` bit 2 is set, else 1 for low HP, else 0. So the KO face shows when bit 2 is set during the update and the HP-changed event fires. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-09, session 20; not checked live. To verify: read `gauge+0x20` live while a Sora is down.
- **The INFO bar call checks no object:** (L194): Confirmed: Gummi mode has no bar object, see the row `*(exe+0xABE828)` in Gummi mode. Lead: `exe+0x17B710(text)` passes `*(exe+0xABE828)` straight to `exe+0x18DEA0` / `exe+0x18DCD0`, which write at object+0x40 and on, so with no bar it would write near 0. Our callers check IN_FIELD first, which is 0 there too. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-07 (TODO 5.2). To verify: step through `exe+0x17B710` on a bench.
- **Where the INFO bar comes from:** (L205): Confirmed: no bar object in Gummi mode, see the row `*(exe+0xABE828)` in Gummi mode. `*(exe+0xABE828)` is a `dk::INFORMATION_WINDOW` (0x4B0 bytes), made by `exe+0x17B1D0` with three siblings (`exe+0xABE820`, `dk::NEXT_EXP` `exe+0xABE830`, `dk::GET_MUNNY` `exe+0xABE838`) from the HUD allocator (`*(exe+0xABCD20)` +0x28), freed by `exe+0x17B080`; both run only from the room start's HUD build `exe+0x157BB0` and the room end `exe+0x1579D0`. Showing it also needs the room's 2D table `exe+0x9BAB08` and the HUD sprite image `*(exe+0xABD240)` (made by `exe+0x16C3A0`); the HUD scheduler (`exe+0x166950`) updates and draws it. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-07 (TODO 5.2). To verify: break on `exe+0x17B1D0`.
- **XInput stick read: remap table and thresholds:** (L213): Confirmed: the circle-to-square stretch, see section 1 `exe+0x133480`. Lead: the byte step `exe+0x133E00` -> `exe+0x132B50` uses a remappable half-axis table (default 0xFF = the natural axes); thresholds at `exe+0x5AAA38` / `exe+0x5AAA58` (±0.01575) and `exe+0x5AAA3C` / `exe+0x5A9CD4` (±0.2). Steam Input pads (gamepad +0x4C = 1) take another path, not read. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-07. To verify: read those floats live.
- **The stick dead zone's floats and the mouse camera:** (L214): Confirmed: the dead zone (60 of 127.5 each axis), see sections 1 and 2 (`exe+0x39C180`, `*(exe+0xBF3338)`). Lead: the floats go to `exe+0xBF3270` + 0x20 / + 0x30, which `exe+0x39C720` copies into the processed entry `exe+0xBF31A0`. With mouse camera input (`exe+0x4E51F0`) the right-stick bytes are rewritten and both lengths scaled by (short × 0.02 + 1). Source: Ghidra 2026-11-07. To verify: read `exe+0xBF31A0` live with the mouse camera.
- **The INFO bar's scroll:** (L222): Confirmed: live values, see the row `*(exe+0xABE828)` scroll. The update of `dk::INFORMATION_WINDOW` is `exe+0x18DF70`. Once the bar has opened (+0x49C bit 1), +0x4A0 (180.0 at each show, `exe+0x18DDE0`) counts down by the frame step: a pause, not how long the bar stays. At 0 the text's x +0x240 (f32) drops by frame step × 0.5 each frame until below +0x498 (i32, that x minus the text's width), then the bar closes (+0x49C bit 0). Text clip at +0x270 (`exe+0x160BD0`): u16 x0, x1, y0, y1; x1 = the group's +0x20 value - 0xAA - 1. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-08 (TODO 5.2). To verify: watch +0x240 and +0x4A0 during one scroll.
- **The item and Cure target list stops at 3:** (L271): The menu build `exe+0x3F29E0` refills page 4 (`menu+0x228`, the target list) every frame: GetPartyMember(n) at `exe+0x3F2A58` (return `exe+0x3F2A5D`) for n = 0..2 (`cmp esi,3` at `exe+0x3F2ABB`), each live member stored at `menu+0xB80 + count*8`. That array holds 3: `+0xB98` is page 8's 0x14-byte target infos, written in the same build. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-11. To verify: a 4th row added after the build, on a bench.
- **Picking a target on page 4:** (L272): The confirm step `exe+0x3F22E0` (vtable `exe+0x5C4C68` + 0x20) takes the command from the previous page's row and the actor from `menu+0xB80[cursor]`, makes a 0x14-byte target info with `exe+0x3BEE60(&info, actor, 0)` and calls `exe+0x3B22D0`. A row is added by `exe+0x3B0F20` (no bounds check; 8 rows per page), its name command from `exe+0x3E60F0(charId)` (0x2A Sora). Source: Ghidra 2026-11-11. To verify: a 4th row picked on a bench.
- **Cure heals party members 0 to 2 only:** (L273): `exe+0x42FA20` is the return address inside `trap_sysobj_party` (1/93, `exe+0x42FA10`), shared by every script that asks for a party member. The CURE scripts loop over 3 party members near their target (a literal 3), so with a full party a Cure aimed at the copy likely doesn't heal it. Source: Ghidra and an OpenKH script decode 2026-11-11. To verify: a Cure on the copy with two companions, on a bench.
- **The pad's left and right bits:** (L275): The raw-to-logical table `exe+0x5C3420` ({raw u64, logical u64} pairs, read by `exe+0x39C720`) maps raw 0x80 and 0x400000 to logical 0x40, raw 0x20 and 0x100000 to 0x80 (bytes read): left and right, likely from both the d-pad and the stick, so pressed & 0xC0 catches both. The generic list `exe+0x2F9440` uses 0x40 / 0x80 as page left / right. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-11 (title switch-menu research). To verify: left/right logged on a bench.

### Downed, Game Over and Mickey

- **Continue after a Game Over restores the form from the room's entry:** (L8): In one two-PC run the friend had entered 04/1A in Wisdom and Reverted there; after losing to Data Xaldin and choosing Continue, its Sora was built as Wisdom (form byte 2 already set when the load started). Probably the game's own checkpoint (the state at the room's entry); not checked without the mod. Source: two-PC log over Steam 2026-10-22 (TODO 1.105). To verify: repeat it without the mod.
- **Mickey's 1-HP floor on bosses:** (L9): In the game Mickey's hits leave a boss at 1 HP; where the game applies it is not found yet. A lead to check: the attack's own flags in the attack table, e.g. a 'can kill a boss' bit. Source: the game's behaviour, Ghidra not done. To verify: find that check in Ghidra.
- **`exe+0x2A10504` is a mode bit word:** (L23): `exe+0x3A5810` sets a bit of `exe+0x2A10504`, `exe+0x3A5820` clears, `exe+0x3A57F0` tests. Bit 4 = Mickey is out (set by his spawn `exe+0x4158C0` / `exe+0x415670`); bit 3 = a minigame-style command mode; the menu build skips the Limit list while bit 1 is set. `exe+0x2A10508` bit 0 is a related one. Source: Ghidra MCP 2026-10-21, session 39. To verify: read bit 4 live while Mickey is out.
- **How a Game Over ends:** (L26): The ctor `exe+0x3FC7A0(obj, ?, kind)` rolls Mickey, ORs 2 into `exe+0x2A11400`, pauses the world, sets bit 2 of `exe+0xABB878` and runs the update `exe+0x3FCEC0` until it returns 0; the cleanup clears those bits, frees it and sets `exe+0x2AE8050` = 0. With no Mickey, `exe+0x3A3460` ends the room's mission. Fade-in is `exe+0x156C90(fade, frames, white)`. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-18 (TODO 1.3), nothing run yet. To verify: read them in a Game Over.
- **Sora's death and revive:** (L83): Confirmed: the revive `exe+0x3AA8D0(actor)`, see its table row. Death = player class vtable +0xB0 `exe+0x3A7330`: `exe+0x3FD5A0(move = *(actor+0xDC0))` turns movement off (move+0xB3C bit 0 cleared, +0x24 bit 0 set, +0xC30 bit 0 cleared, +0xC40 = 0). The revive (only caller the script-call wrapper `exe+0x42E1E0`, Healing Light's presumably) clears actor+0x9B8 bit 2, calls `exe+0x3B4DA0(actor, 0)` and `exe+0x3FD5F0(move)` = movement on (those bits back, +0xC40 = 0), then sets HP to max. After our old revive the Mickey loser's movement stayed off (move+0xB3C 1 -> 0, +0xC30 3 -> 2). Source: disassembly 2026-10-18, session 38. To verify: read the move fields after a revive.
- **The player's own gauge, its KO face and the low-HP beep:** (L84): Confirmed: the hook's effect, see F23. Built by the HUD build `exe+0x16C3A0` (ctor `exe+0x16B6F0`), pointer `exe+0xABD248` (friend gauges `exe+0xABD250` / `exe+0xABD258`); vtable `exe+0x5B2100`, +0x08 update `exe+0x181270` (the friend gauge's `exe+0x182730`); gauge+0x40 = the actor. The update first runs `exe+0x17EE60` (KO from actor+0x9B8 bit 2), then the beep (byte `exe+0xAD8040`): dead and on -> stop; low HP and off -> start; on and not low -> stop; a hidden HUD also stops it. So the dead bit set only for this update shows the KO face and stops the beep, nothing else sees it. Source: disassembly 2026-10-18, session 38. To verify: read `exe+0xAD8040` live at low HP.
- **Old crash address `exe+0x1CCCF6`:** (L115): `exe+0x1CCCF6` was the host's crash when Mickey's rescue ended and the game rebuilt the party with the copy in Donald's slot as an AI member, in the old slot mode (removed in session 35). Kept from TODO.md. Source: crash, session 11. To verify: only if a copy goes into a party slot again.

### Cutscenes and cameras

- **Boss in-fight scenes ('re_b_...'):** (L65): `exe+0x3AD2D0(event)` plays an event through StartCutscene `exe+0x2CC4B0(event[2], event+0x60, ...)` (the hooked one) and makes it the current event `exe+0x2A11478`. `exe+0x3DB2F0(actor, char* name, target)` plays the actor's own event by name: the name (up to 4 chars) found as entry type 0x16 in the actor's file, a 0xC0 event built (`exe+0x152430` alloc), event+0x10 = actor+0x80, `target` bound. Script-command form: `exe+0x3D8CF0` (handles decoded with `exe+0x4AD270`). Idea, not tried: the host sends the name when event+0x10 = a synced boss + 0x80; the friend calls `exe+0x3DB2F0` on its bound copy. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-12, session 28 (TODO 1.20). To verify: try that idea on a bench.
- **Cutscene skip and timer addresses (Volpestyle):** (L70): Confirmed: menu byte `exe+0x7435D0` = 0x08 in cutscene pauses, see F128. Volpestyle's CUTSCENE_TIMER `exe+0xB64F18`, CUTSCENE_SKIP `exe+0xB64F1C`, CUTSCENE_LEN `exe+0xB64F34` stayed 0 through a scene, its pause and a skip in one live check: wrong or unused on this build (the skip byte is `exe+0xB64F9C`, F129). PAUSE_STATUS `exe+0xABB7F8` not checked. Source: Volpestyle `docs/pointer_map_v1.md`, TODO 5.3. To verify: PAUSE_STATUS in a pause.
- **Scene abort `exe+0x2CC490`:** (L71): `exe+0x2CC490` is `xor ecx,ecx; mov byte [exe+0xB64FA1],0; mov dword [exe+0xB65210],4; jmp exe+0x2CC0B0`: it ends the scene with no fade. Source: byte scan of the exe, session 27 (Ghidra had no function there). To verify: call it during a scene on a bench.
- **Cutscene runtime:** (L90): Confirmed: start hook, handler convention, actor table, slots, see the rows 'Cutscene start', 'Cutscene command handlers', 'Cutscene actor table'. `exe+0x2CC4B0(script, ...)` copies the scene name to `exe+0xB65578` and starts tasks `exe+0x2CC180` (setup), `exe+0x2CB5D0` (walker), `exe+0x2CC8A0`. Actor table `*(exe+0xB653E0)`, lookup `exe+0x2CB300(id)`. SeqPlayAnimation `exe+0x2D2160` makes the actor at its first animation via `exe+0x3DF930` (object with bit 30 0x40000000 = event actor, no spawn budget check), and takes a slot with no null check. SeqActorPosition `exe+0x2D0BD0` returns if the actor is missing. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-09, session 20 (TODO 5.12). To verify: fill the slots.
- **Cutscene twin doesn't move:** (L91): Confirmed: the cause (our puppet took the twin for the copy), see F102. Still Ghidra only: SeqPlayAnimation schedules task 0xA7EE (`exe+0x2C8850`, actor id at task+0x18), which frees (`exe+0x2CEE10`) every animation entry in state 4 whose +0xC is that actor id and +8 is 0. Entries carry Sora's actor id, not the twin's, so only Sora's task frees the entries both used. Source: Ghidra (session 22, TODO 5.12). To verify: log the freed entries.
- **A boss's scene camera is a small cutscene aimed through one anchor:** (L156): Confirmed: the shot takes an actor's pose one frame in and keeps it, see F491. Still Ghidra only: `exe+0x3DB2F0(owner, name, target)` plays the owner's shot (`owner+0x928`, type 0x16) as the event camera `*(exe+0x2A11478)`; its callback `exe+0x3AC630` re-aims: 1 = the player `*(exe+0x2A105D0)`, 2 = the boss anchor `*(exe+0x2AE7D30)`. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-28, session 46 (TODO 1.62). To verify: which call sets the base one frame in.
- **trap_camera_cancel (1/223) ends the scene:** (L181): `trap_camera_cancel` (1/223) = `exe+0x430BE0` calls `exe+0x2CB4F0(args[0] != 0)`, which, when a scene runs and isn't `nm_event_112`, sets the scene state `exe+0xB65210` to 4 and ends the scene (fade, back to the field). A grabber's camera start runs as a scene ('re_n_bb050-00' in one friend log), so the cancel ends it. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-02 (TODO 1.120 / 1.170). To verify: watch `exe+0xB65210` when a grabber cancels.
- **What moves a boss shot's camera each frame:** (L282): Confirmed: the base matrix pointer `exe+0xB64F90`, see F491. Ghidra only: `exe+0x2D37B0` multiplies the shot's eye and look-at keys by that matrix and writes the eye `exe+0x740A88`, look-at `exe+0x740A98`, roll `exe+0x740AA8` and fov `exe+0x740AAC` while camera mode `exe+0xAC1528` is 1. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-11 (TODO 1.179). To verify: compare the eye with the picture on a bench.

- **A scene holds a Keyblade with command 0x0A kind 1:** (L304): Confirmed: the hold, the effect tie and the effect table's id and tie fields, see F524, F525. Still open: a later SeqActorPosition on a held actor writes its offset (L176); whether a scene lets go of a held actor isn't checked. Source: Ghidra and all 951 `ard/us` scenes, 2026-11-12. To verify: eh04 108 or tt08 907 (a held Keyblade moved) with the twin.
- **Scene effects are found by their effect id:** (L305): Confirmed: an effect copied under a new id plays on the twin, see F525. Ghidra only: SeqEffect +4 frame, +8 id, +0x0A pax, +0x0C entry, +0x0E kind; kind 0 plays (`exe+0x2D9340`), storing the effect at the tied actor's +0x2A0; kinds 1-3, 5, 6 act on the playing effect; rows found by id, first match (`exe+0x2D95A0`), no null check; all freed at once (`exe+0x2D9680`, from the per-scene clear `exe+0x2C85B0`). StopEffect 0x3F names no id. Source: Ghidra, 2026-11-12. To verify: a twin effect skipped by our guard, in a log.

### Title, saves and story progress

- **Soft reset during a room change crashes:** (L63): In one two-PC run the friend picked 'Title Screen', then its game asked for a room change; our soft reset `exe+0x154DB0` ran and it crashed at `exe+0x39DA55` (release list `*(exe+0x29F33C8)` empty). Reading: both teardowns ran. `exe+0x39DA10(x)` lowers the count of the entry whose `+0x58` is x; with an empty list it writes to address 2 (Ghidra). Fixed in our DLL (TODO 6.11). Source: two-PC log 2026-10-11. To verify: reproduce it without the fix.
- **Save container: the decrypt and the buffer:** (L78): Confirmed: the opening, path, the swap of `exe+0x79CB10` and the writer `exe+0x100810`, see F116. `exe+0xFEF80` reads the whole PNG (deletes an empty file), decrypts the save chunk with a key from the player's Steam ID and copies it to `+0x10`: 0x10 header, slot count x 0x158 slot info, then the slots. A second container at `exe+0x79CD38` is likely the system file. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-10. To verify: dump `+0x10` after boot.
- **Title menu code:** (L79): Confirmed: the Theater check, the menu input `exe+0x2B35A0` and the LOAD path (a picked save sets `exe+0xBEDF60`), see F114. Ghidra only: `exe+0x2B4590` title main loop, `exe+0x2B4C60` title start (loads `title.2ld` or `titlejf.2ld`), `exe+0x2B3C10` closes the menu; `exe+0x14EAD0` = bit 25 of `exe+0x749804`, which picks `titlejf.2ld` and a 2-row menu (0 here). Source: Ghidra 2026-10-10. To verify: set that bit on a bench.
- **Save file reading and writing (Volpestyle):** (L80): Confirmed: the opening `exe+0xFEF80` and the writer `exe+0x100810`, see F116. Not checked: it writes through `exe+0x145170`; boot `exe+0x101000` can call the deleting helper `exe+0x1007A0` after init errors or an account/header mismatch; `exe+0x3E38D0` swaps two selector bytes of the SAVE party row; `exe+0x3C2120` commits status bytes into a SAVE character record. Source: another project (Volpestyle), 2026-10-02. To verify: break on them.
- **Volpestyle's progress allow-list:** (L107): Confirmed: the synced ranges (`save+0x10` programs, `+0x1C90` story flags, `+0x22F8` visited, `+0x23AC` chests), see section 3. Theirs: chest bit 0 and bits 412-415 unknown; their host sends the ranges, the client writes them before its room loads; never synced: characters `+0x24F0`, inventory `+0x3580`, forms, munny, EXP. It skips the progress script's writes (L133). Source: another project (Volpestyle), 2026-10-25. To verify: test chest bit 0.
- **What a story flag's setter also writes:** (L133): `exe+0x39AFC0(id)` sets the bit, then runs the flag's script from `00progress.bin` (table `exe+0xBF30B0`[world]): op 1 room programs, op 2-5 u16 pairs at `save+0x1EF8` + 4n, op 6 a per-world byte `save+0x23EC`, op 7 clears flags, op 8/9 set/clear bits at `save+0x2390`, op 0xC more flags, op 0xD per-world record `save+0x3FEC`; then auto-flags (`exe+0x3F0EA0`), visited rooms, bits at `save+0x1EF0`, `save+0x3724` and `save+0x4038` (queue `save+0x4058`). None of the ones read touches items or stats. `+0x1EF0`, `+0x2390`, `+0x4038` only grow across 14 dumps; meaning not known. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-25 (TODO 5.1). To verify: diff the save around one flag on a bench.
- **Treasure table, item add and chest take: the rest:** (L134): Confirmed: CommitLocation `exe+0x152BE0`, the treasure table `exe+0x2A0F7A0`, the item add `exe+0x3C3F00` and the chest take `exe+0x3A1B60`, see their rows (bench 2026-10-25). Ghidra only: the table lookup is `exe+0x3A19C0(world, room, index)`, a chest's opened-test `exe+0x3A1EA0`; the chest take fills an 8-entry 'obtained' ring at `exe+0x2A0F6D8` and, when `exe+0x2A105D0` is set, gives the item add that character's id instead of 100. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-25. To verify: open a chest with `exe+0x2A105D0` set.
- **The chest object's opening and the item window:** (L135): Confirmed: `exe+0x4025C0`, `exe+0x159550`, `exe+0x3B0CC0`, `exe+0x3E39A0`, `actor+0xC00` and `actor+0x120`, see their rows (bench 2026-10-25). Ghidra only: the chest setup `exe+0x402200` / `exe+0x4024E0` picks motion 0x97 or 0x98 by the opened-test `exe+0x3A1EA0`; `exe+0x40A500` writes an (0x20, -1) entry into an 8-entry list at `actor+0x540`; the ring `exe+0x2A0F6D8` (+0 name text, +8 second text, +0x10 icon) is read by `exe+0x3A1B20`. The gold 'OBTAINED' heading looks like a picture, not text: not confirmed. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-25 (TODO 4.6). To verify: read `actor+0x540` after a chest opens.
- **Clearing the Auto Limit between the save pick and the first room:** (L157): Confirmed: the clear itself and the Game Over load, see F388. The save list sets `exe+0xBEDF60` when a save is picked (`exe+0x2E5460` case 0x16); the save's copy into `exe+0x9A98B0` and the stat sheets' build come after, before in-field `exe+0x9BA8D0` turns 1 again. 13 records of 0x114 from `save+0x24F0` to the Drive Forms at `+0x32F4`; only ids 1-3 verified. Source: Ghidra 2026-10-29 (TODO 1.139). To verify: check the other ids on a bench.
- **OpenKH's names for our save rows:** (L184): `save+0x2444` = `Timer`, int[21] (one per world + 2): the counters that grow 60 a second are timers, which is which not checked. Character record +0x12 `ItemCount`, +0x34 `Items` (8 x s16), +0x44 `ItemAutoReload` (8 x s16), so `+0x3A` = item slot 3. Treasure row +0xA = `OverallChestIndex` (our bit in `save+0x23AC`); item row u16 +4 = `StatEntry` (our ability bit). `BdxTraps.cs` names {1, 27} `trap_area_event_set` (L57: a getter). Source: OpenKH (`SaveDataFinalMix.cs`, `SaveCharacter.cs`, `Trsr.cs`, `Item.cs`), 2026-11-03. To verify: watch which timer grows in which world.
- **Save slots in the save file:** (L242): World +0x0C, room +0x0D, spawn +0x0E; the first slot's magic `KH2J` is at file 0x19690, stride 0x10FC0, 76 saves in the bench copy. A position in the file isn't the game's slot number (game slot 96 is probably position 63, the only World Map save listed). Source: OpenKH `SaveDataFinalMix.cs`, read on the bench copy 2026-11-09. To verify: load a slot and compare.
- **The title menu reads no left or right:** (L274): The menu input `exe+0x2B35A0` tests only 0x10, 0x20 and 0x4 (0x404 on the press-start screen) and runs only while the title's sub-mode `exe+0xB1D5EC` is 0. The idle timer `exe+0xB1D5F4` resets only while the menu is busy or on pressed & 0x34; at 5100 it starts the intro movie. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-11 (title switch-menu research). To verify: left/right and the timer read on a bench.
- **How a title row is redrawn:** (L276): The row's layout group comes from `exe+0x2B3D20`: table `exe+0x5B8620` {0, 1, 0x12} for 3 rows, `exe+0x5B8624` {2, 3, 4, 0x13} for 4 (bytes read), picked by the Theater flag. A cursor move calls `exe+0x2EC550(rows layout, group, -1)` and sound `exe+0x2EB740(7, 1)`; the main loop re-applies it at `exe+0x2B48FC`. `exe+0x2B3400` sets flag 1 and count 4 only while the flag is 0. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-11 (title switch-menu research). To verify: a page switch on a bench.
- **Tz::Layout fields used by the title:** (L277): vtable `exe+0x5B1DC0`: +0xE0 current group, +0xE4 next group (-1 none), +0xEC pending restart (-10 none), +0x48 its own texture slots (0x100 bytes each, handle at +0x48), +0x50 the sequence set whose +0x50 is an array of 0x1A0-byte sequences, sequence +8 the sprite table (`exe+0x196010`; a sprite is 0x2C bytes, UV at +4). `exe+0x166E20(obj)` tells whether an object is still in the task list. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-11 (title switch-menu research). To verify: read on a bench with the menu up.
- **The key-icon swap touches one layout:** (L278): `exe+0x2B40D0` reads the icon style `exe+0x715380`, caches it in `exe+0x73FD08` and writes the sheet's handle into slot 8 of the background layout `*(exe+0xB1D5D0)` only, so the open menu's rows likely always draw sheet 8. This conflicts with the old note that a bench drew `-9.png` (F115's history). Source: Ghidra 2026-11-11 (title switch-menu research). To verify: different marks in `-8.png` and `-9.png` on a bench.
- **What NEW GAME and THEATER run:** (L279): NEW GAME sets sub-mode 1; on the difficulty sub-menu's Yes (`exe+0x2B7420` returns 1) the main loop sets action 0, calls close `exe+0x2B3C10`, then `exe+0x2B88C0` (difficulty into the save) and `exe+0x3E4260(2)`. THEATER sets sub-mode 2, loads `menu/eventviewer.2ld` and runs `exe+0x2B9A20`. Close has two callers; only NEW GAME plays the labels' exit. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-11 (title switch-menu research). To verify: a NEW GAME on a bench.
- **The title's extra sheets and Panacea:** (L280): title start `exe+0x2B4C60` registers `title_2ld9/10/11.png` with `exe+0x12EB70`; Panacea serves a mod PNG only under a name the game's list has, and a raw `title.2ld` cuts that list to the BAR's 9 textures. Sheet 0 is not free: `exe+0x2B64E0` / `exe+0x2B6940` draw group 9 with it. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-11 and OpenKH's Panacea source (b67a743). To verify: Panacea's log on a bench.
- **The title sheets' free space:** (L281): per a file read, the free area common to all languages is about 120x41 (x 7-126, y 154-194) plus 80x23 (x 47-126, y 195-217), smaller than F113 says; the Japanese labels run to y 245 as the lit row's subtitle; the base `-8` and `-10` are 1024 px, `-9` and `-11` 512 px. Source: file read 2026-11-11 (title switch-menu research). To verify: re-measure in all 7 languages, then rewrite F113.

### Gummi Ship and World Map

- **The Gummi flight's state:** (L193): Confirmed: `NOW` 0F/00, IN_FIELD 0 and no room load in Gummi mode, see the table `exe+0x9BA8D0`. Volpestyle's door 4 (menu) and door 13 (back on the map) were not seen here (`exe+0x71700A`). Unchecked: the first Land of Dragons trip needs the Asteroid Sweep route; our follow waits out a friend's flight. Source: another project (Volpestyle), 2026-11-07, and a probe run 2026-11-07. To verify: a friend's first Land of Dragons trip.
- **The Gummi files:** (L195): `gumibattle/` holds the flight's tables (`enemyparam`, `playerparam`, `rank`, `treasure`, `systemparam`, `planset`, `lockonparam`, `missileparam`...), `gumimenu/` the menus and the editor (`gumimenu.bar`, `gumiedit.bar`, `planinfo.bar`, `gumiblockdata.bin`), and there are 27 `.gbx` mission maps with no parser. Source: another project (OpenKH `docs/kh2/files.md`), 2026-11-07. To verify: open the files with OpenKH's tools.
- **The .gbx mission file layout:** (L196): Header `GBX_`, +0x08 the rail's offset (0x60), +0x0C a 0x60-byte section (not decoded), +0x10 the mission data, +0x14 its size, +0x40 the route's `.map` name. Rail: 28-byte points (position, angles, distance so far), the same in a route's 3 missions. Mission data: 8 (offset, count) pairs; table 0 = 20-byte triggers (u16 index x2, f32 distance, f32 end or 0, u32, i32 -1); table 1 = 184-byte enemies (u16 index, u16 trigger, +0x08 world position, +0x14 angles, +0x40 kind 512-1425); tables 2-6 not decoded. Source: decoded from the game files, 2026-11-07 (OpenKH has no parser). To verify: match a trigger's distance to a live flight's rail distance.
- **The mission's file name and its two globals:** (L197): Confirmed: the route, the mission and EX, see the table `exe+0x9A8788`, `exe+0x716C00`, `exe+0x9A878C`. `exe+0x1E1A10` builds `gumibattle/gm%02d%c.gbx` from the route (get `exe+0x151B20`) and the mission (set `exe+0x151B50(n)`: n < 1 is 1, n kept at `0x716C04`, 1-3 stored) with the letters 1 a, 2 b, 3 c. EX is set by `exe+0x151B40`. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-07. To verify: the getters and setters on a bench.
- **World Map to Gummi mode, and the mission list:** (L198): The key door's World menu ends in `exe+0x398810` (L215): when `this+0x354` is 1 (Proceed) it queues `exe+0x3946F0(this+0x350 - 1)`, whose task sets the route and switches mode. The mission list's input `exe+0x24E9E0`: confirm (pad bit 4) sets EX and mission (cursor + 1) and moves the menu `*(exe+0xAF9E00)` to state 2 (`exe+0x2435B0`); cancel (bit 8) goes back. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-07. To verify: Proceed and a mission confirm on a bench.
- **The Gummi mission records in the save:** (L199): The Gummi mission records sit at save +0x100E0 (`exe+0x9B9990`), 0x40 bytes each, record route * 3 + mission - 1 (EX from record 27; `exe+0x2C76F0`): +0x1B a rank, +0x1E a flag (cleared or open), +0x20 f32 (high score?). They lie outside the areas story sync copies (up to +0x4038). Source: Ghidra 2026-11-07. To verify: read a record after a cleared mission on a bench.
- **The game's own class names for the Gummi Ship:** (L200): Flight `gb::`: PATH, SCROLL, GENERATOR, SUB_GENERATOR, GUMISHIP, PLAYER, FRIEND, FRIEND_FORMATION, SCORE, START_EVENT, GOAL_EVENT, DEAD_EVENT, REBIRTH_EVENT, MENU_RESULT. Menus `gm::`: TOP, MISSION, SELECTBODY, LASTCHECK, GUMIEDIT, GUMIFACTORY, GUMIDATA, TINYSELECT. Map: `worldmap::` and `dk::WM_*` (WM_GLANDING, WM_GLANDING_INFO, `worldmap::MessageWidget`). Vftables: `gb::SCROLL` `exe+0x5B4E48`, `gb::FRIEND` `exe+0x5B5FB0`, `gb::FRIEND_FORMATION` `exe+0x5B6018`. Source: RTTI in Ghidra, 2026-11-07. To verify: read a live object's vftable in a flight.
- **The flight runs on time:** (L201): Confirmed: one `gb::SCROLL` at `exe+0x729740`, its +0x14 progress and +0xD0 states 0-3, see the table. Ghidra only: +0x08 the rail, +0x10 its point count; point = progress / `*(exe+0xAEA110)` (2 fit one run's end); +0xD0 5 at the rail's end (not seen). Enemies come from triggers along the rail (L196), so two flights started together stay in step: the host's +0x14 is the value to sync. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-07. To verify: +0xD0 5 at a rail's end.
- **The Gummi menu's screens and the launch:** (L202): The menu `*(exe+0xAF9E00)` goes to screen n with `exe+0x2435B0(n)`, back with `exe+0x2434D0`. Screens: 0 `gm::TOP` `exe+0x24E3E0`, 1 `gm::MISSION` `exe+0x24ED50`, 2 `gm::BODYSELECT` `exe+0x24F230`, 3 `gm::TINYSELECT` `exe+0x24F710`, 4 `gm::LASTCHECK` `exe+0x24FE00`, 5 `gm::GUMIFACTORY` `exe+0x250430`, 6 `gm::FBODYSELECT` `exe+0x250B70`, 7 `gm::FTINYSELECT` `exe+0x251020`. LASTCHECK's loop `exe+0x24FFB0` uses the flags at `exe+0xAFC0C4`: bit 1 the first confirm, bit 0 Start and the launch animation, bit 3 go (the flight starts through the mode object `exe+0x716C10`, vtable slot 1); Abort goes back. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-07. To verify: each screen's number while moving through the menu.
- **How a flight's triggers and enemies start:** (L203): Confirmed: the scroll's +0x18 is the rail distance, see the table. Ghidra only: the scroll update `exe+0x1E8A30` turns +0x14 into +0x18. The zone manager `exe+0x1EE780` (while its +0x7C is 1) fires each trigger (L196) once the scroll's +0x18 reaches its distance; one whose byte +0x0E is 0 runs the zone builder `exe+0x1EC470`: a `gb::ZONE` per trigger and a `gb::GENERATOR` per enemy whose bytes +0x23 / +0x24 (min / max) hold the mission number `exe+0x151AE0` = `*(exe+0x716C04)` (0 read as 1). Runtime tables: triggers +0x10 (count +0x18), enemies +0x20, cameras +0x40, alerts +0x50, fades +0x60, EX +0x70. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-07. To verify: a generator's mission bytes.
- **The World Map's choices may be field commands:** (L204): In one probe run on the World Map, commands 0x247, 0x246 and 0xB6 (type 0x206) went through the command executor; each of the two Proceeds followed a 0xB6, and entities were frozen (`exe+0x2A171E8` 1) from a 0xB6 to the next 0xB6 or the Proceed. OpenKH names none of 182, 582, 583. If 0xB6 is the key door's World menu, 5.4's command hold could catch it. Source: bench log 2026-11-07 (TODO 5.2). To verify: hold 0xB6 and see if the World menu waits.
- **The Gummi menu's help bar:** (L206): Confirmed: some parts, see sections 1 and 2 `exe+0x2437B0`, `*(exe+0xAF9F00)`, `exe+0xAF9FB0`. Gummi mode's start makes it; its task compares the screen name each frame. Text path: `exe+0x252140` → `exe+0x271850` → `exe+0x2515D0(window + 0x1E0, id)`. Callers: each cursor move (`exe+0x274850`, help id at list+0x58, unchecked) and the mission screen. Source: Ghidra and `msg/us/gumi.bar`, 2026-11-07 (TODO 5.2). To verify: the help id at list+0x58.
- **The Gummi menu's start and end:** (L207): Confirmed: the alive rule, see section 2 `*(exe+0xAF9FA0)`. The menu mode's handler `exe+0x243A10` (0 = start, 1 = end) and the end alone `exe+0x2438F0` tear the menu down (windows `exe+0x251800`, tasks `exe+0x1500D0`), then zero `*(exe+0xAF9FA0)` and the pool (`exe+0x2513D0`). `exe+0x243690` also sets `*(exe+0xAF9FA0)` (no direct caller). Source: Ghidra 2026-11-07 (TODO 5.2). To verify: the teardown when the menu closes.
- **The Gummi menu's windows:** (L208): Confirmed: opening one, see section 1 `exe+0x2529A0`. `exe+0x2529A0(type)` allocates (from `*(exe+0xAFB420)` when given 0; vftable `exe+0x5B7558`: 0 delete, 2 update, 3 draw) and inits by type from `gumimenu/menuentry.bar`. `exe+0x251C40` makes a scheduler task on `*(exe+0xAFB4E0)`, unchecked; `exe+0x271310` plays the close. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-07 (TODO 5.2). To verify: the scheduler task on `*(exe+0xAFB4E0)`.
- **The help task's tables:** (L209): A letter per screen at `exe+0x7358B8` (top, mission, factory 'o'; collection 'g'; tutorial 'b'), a look per letter at `exe+0x7358C0` (o 0x15A, g 0x15B, b 0x15C, p 0x15D), transitions at `exe+0x7358E0` (from, to, look 0x15E-0x163). It opens a window only when +8 is 0, keeps one across help screens (re-typed) and closes it with the look of the screen where it opened one. `exe+0x2515D0` with a negative id stores 0 (an empty bar). Source: Ghidra 2026-11-07 (TODO 5.2). To verify: the look when moving from the mission screen to the collection.
- **Moving a Gummi window:** (L210): Confirmed: some parts, see sections 1 and 3 `exe+0x252B00`, window +0x3B4 / +0x3B8. The offset is 0 at creation; each frame the update puts parent position + offset at +0x3BC / +0x3C0 and the draw copies it to its `YI::SEQUENCE` (+0x40) at +0x18C / +0x190 (not drawn outside x -0x200..0x400, y -0x1A0..0x340). `exe+0x252B00` calls `exe+0x251C40(w, flag, priority)`. `exe+0x251B60(window)` = in the live list `*(exe+0xAFB4B8)` and not marked for deletion (+0x10 bit 0). Record (`menuentry.bar`): +0x40 layout name, +0x60 sequence, +0x64 layout index, +0x68..+0x7C animations, +0x80 default message id. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-07 (TODO 5.2). To verify: the off-screen limits.
- **Scaling a Gummi window at runtime:** (L211): Confirmed: some parts, see section 3 (window +0x128 / +0x1B8, +0x288 / +0x1E4, +0x140). The draw `exe+0x192250` multiplies each animation's keyframe scale by seq +0x178 (all), +0x17C (x), +0x180 (y), floats set to 1.0 only at type init `exe+0x270930`, or uses them alone with ScalingDisable (0x40). Text: `exe+0x3AEED0(1, text, x, y, color, size, align)`, scale = size / the font's base, placed at the window offset (+ the parent's) + the anchor seq +0x120 / +0x124, + the group's layout x / y when +0x288 bit 4: a window has no text offset of its own. Icon slots: anchor + (+0x354, +0x35C), string +0x330. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-07 (TODO 5.2). To verify: change seq +0x178 on a live window.
- **The Gummi help bar's layout data:** (L212): Entry 0 of `gumimenu/us/gumimenu.bar` is the 2LD `gummi_menu_lay`, in the 512 x 416 2D space. Sequence 4: the orange help bar's groups 92 / 94 (close / open) and 95 (idle: body sprite group 79 at (256, 376), Dale's portrait 77 at the same point, a text marker at (216, 376)), text size 16. Groups 84 / 89 / 90 are window type 0x263 `EXMISSION_SELECT` (close 84, open 89, idle 90): a small box at (422, 34), text marker (375, 27), text size 14; `exe+0x270930` gives it a button icon slot. Worked out, not measured: the help bar centers its text on x 216 and the EX box holds about 14 letters. Source: the game file read in OpenKH's field order, 2026-11-07 (TODO 5.2). To verify: a 14-letter EX text.
- **The key door's World menu:** (L215): Confirmed: some parts, see section 1 `exe+0x398810`, `exe+0x3946F0`. `dk::WM_GLANDING` is made by `exe+0x398BD0(route + 1)` (callers `exe+0x394FC0`, `exe+0x395730`), the open one at `*(exe+0xBF2FC0)`. Proceed (item 0) sets +0x354 = 1 and +0x20 bit 0 and closes the info panel (`exe+0x399CD0`); a cancel or another item sets +0x354 = 0. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-08 (TODO 5.2). To verify: +0x354 after Proceed and cancel.
- **The Gummi menu's top screen:** (L216): Confirmed: the items and Start's two ways, see F452. `gm::TOP` (vftable `exe+0x5B6AF0`, loop `exe+0x24E4E0`; list at +0x10). Start goes to screen 2 when record (route, 1) +0x1D is 0. Gummi Editor: screen 5 when `exe+0x243790` (item 0xAC owned) is true, else 6. World Map: a yes / no box (message 0x4AB0); Yes calls `exe+0x153160`: world 0x0F, room 0, door `*(exe+0x5B15E8 + route * 4)`. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-08 (TODO 5.2). To verify: screen 5.
- **Which missions a route lists:** (L217): Confirmed: the records and the confirm's calls, see sections 1 and 2. `exe+0x24EE60` lists 2 missions, or 3 when record (route, 3) +0x1E is set; records come from `exe+0x2C76F0(save, route, n, ex)`.
