- **The launch's confirm is one function:** (L218): Confirmed: the flags, see section 2 `exe+0xAFC0C4`. `exe+0x24FEB0`: Start calls `exe+0x252E50(model, 0xD)` (the launch motion) and closes the list. Direct writers of the flags: `exe+0x243BC0` (0), `exe+0x252E50` (bit 2), `exe+0x24FFB0`, `exe+0x24FEB0`, `exe+0x250270` (clears bit 1). Source: Ghidra 2026-11-08 (TODO 5.2). To verify: bit 2 during the launch motion.
- **The Gummi menus' list selector:** (L219): Confirmed: the return values and the hold, see section 1 `exe+0x274850`. Vftable `exe+0x5B7658`, the update is slot 1; confirm mask +0xC0; it also returns -1 on a disabled item (+0xA8 + cursor set); active +0xE4 bit 0. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-08 (TODO 5.2). To verify: a confirm on a disabled item.
- **The Gummi menu's screen stack:** (L220): Confirmed: the index and the landing on top, see section 2 `*(exe+0xAF9E00)`. Entries of 8 bytes (+0 screen, +4 saved cursor); `exe+0x243580(menu, this screen)` pushes going forward. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-08 (TODO 5.2). To verify: the saved cursor after going back.
- **Ship selection and the mission list's loop:** (L221): Confirmed: the input and the processed pad, see section 1 `exe+0x278CB0`, `exe+0x39B780`. `gm::BODYSELECT` (vftable `exe+0x5B6C18`, loop `exe+0x24F2C0`): input 2 = a third button; confirm goes to screen 3 (L223) or 4 (launch). `gm::MISSION` (vftable `exe+0x5B6BA0`, loop `exe+0x24EDF0`). The processed pad's +0x18 = pressed with repeat; bit 0x10000 of +8 toggles EX in the list. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-08 (TODO 5.2). To verify: the third button.
- **`tiny`, the map's other label and item counts:** (L223): Ship selection's confirm opens `gm::TINYSELECT` only on page 0 (Original Blueprints), with `exe+0x243790` (item 0xAC owned) and `exe+0x52E080(exe+0x151C80())` > 0. Item count: `exe+0x529070(id)` = byte `exe+0x9BA710 + id` (id 1 is summed from others). A world under the ship (`+0x6C8` negative) gets its label from `exe+0x396A00(byte at *(actor+0xD50) + 4)` in `exe+0x394810`. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-08 (TODO 5.2). To verify: open `tiny` on a bench.
- **The Gummi flight's task and its steps:** (L224): `exe+0x1E1BA0` runs the whole flight; its step is `*(exe+0xAD9474)`. Step 8 flies and watches the ship (`exe+0x1FD150()` = `*(exe+0xAF0540)`): +0x5D8 4 goes to steps 10-11 (`gb::DEAD_EVENT`) then the Game Over list (menu id 0x34); 5 goes to 12-13 (`gb::REBIRTH_EVENT`) and back to 7; the scroll's goal goes to 14-16 (`gb::GOAL_EVENT`, tally `exe+0x210730`), then step 18 opens the list (menu id 0x35). A Retry sets step 0x11 (`exe+0x1E1630`) and step 3 starts over. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-08 (TODO 5.2). To verify: log `*(exe+0xAD9474)` through a Game Over and a goal on a bench.
- **The Gummi flight's menus:** (L225): Confirmed: the list's answers and items, see section 1 `exe+0x2F9440`. One result block `*(exe+0x9A8790)` (`exe+0x1519E0()`): +0 to +2 settings, +3 the result (0 Continue, 1 Retry, 2 Gummi Menu, 3 World Map), +8 the callback run when the menu closes. Pause task `exe+0x2F0E20` (input `exe+0x2EF830`); Game Over and results task `exe+0x2F0A20`; `exe+0x2F6500()` closes the menu. The pause opens from `exe+0x1E2110` on pressed bit 2 at pad +8, ship state 2 and `*(exe+0x9006B0)` 0, menu id 0x33. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-08 (TODO 5.2). To verify: read the block's +3 after each answer on a bench.
- **How a Gummi ship goes down:** (L226): Confirmed: setting the two bits, see section 2 `*(exe+0xAF0540)`. `exe+0x1FE240(ship)` sets +0x140C bit 0x10 when a part is dead (+0x488, or sub-ships +0x688, +0x690); then it blows the ship up and writes +0x5D8: 5 (rebirth) when +0x140C bit 0x80000 is clear and `exe+0x225E30` allows it, else 4. Pause callback `exe+0x1E2080` handles 1 Retry and 0 resume, not 2 and 3. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-08 (TODO 5.2). To verify: read +0x5D8 on a bench.
- **What a Gummi clear writes to the save:** (L227): When the tally `exe+0x210730` opens: Mission 1's first clear sets +0x1E on records (route, 1) and (route, 2); otherwise a rank above 5 opens this mission's EX and a rank above 2 on Missions 1 and 2 opens Mission 3. The closing Cross runs `exe+0x209750`: record +0x1D = 1, then the best scores. Route, mission, EX now: `exe+0x151B20`, `exe+0x151AE0`, `exe+0x151B30`. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-08 (TODO 5.2). To verify: diff the save around a clear.
- **How a flight's ships are made:** (L228): `exe+0x1FCF00` allocates `gb::PLAYER` (0x1410 bytes) and, for filled blueprint slots 1 and 2, up to two `gb::FRIEND` (0x5A0 bytes, constructor `exe+0x22B500`), the second at PLAYER +0x690, both handed to the `gb::FRIEND_FORMATION` at PLAYER +0x698. `exe+0x1FAB30(gumiship, index)` builds a ship's parts from three blueprints: `exe+0xAEF488` (the player's), slot 0's (section 2), `exe+0xAEF4D8` (slot 1). `exe+0x1FA590` fills them from `exe+0x151C80()` (the chosen ship) and `exe+0x151CC0(0 or 1)` (the Teeny Ships, buffers `exe+0x9A9080` + n x 0x400). Source: Ghidra 2026-11-08 (TODO 5.2 stage 2). To verify: read the three blueprints on a bench with Teeny Ships set.
- **A friend ship's place and what's drawn:** (L229): `exe+0x22CEF0(formation)` runs once a frame (guard PLAYER +0x140C bit 0x20000) and writes each friend's +8, +0x28 and +0x38. `exe+0x1E53F0(obj)` builds the matrix at +0x68 from those and the scale +0x58; with +0x1F8 bit 1 it first moves them off the rail's plane. `exe+0x205EE0` (blueprint cleanup) frees +0 and +0x10 and only clears +8. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-08 (TODO 5.2 stage 2). To verify: log a friend's +8 and +0x68 on a bench.
- **What Gummi enemies aim at and what hurts a ship:** (L230): Enemy aim and shot tests get their one target from `exe+0x1FD150()` (returns `*(exe+0xAF0540)`). Five shot hit tests (`exe+0x21EE20`, `exe+0x21FA00`, `exe+0x220390`, `exe+0x220B70`, `exe+0x221650`, likely the five `gb::ENEMY_*` shot classes) damage with `exe+0x1FF340(player, shot+0x44)` (+0x18 the damage). `exe+0x21EE20` tries the friends first: a friend absorbs the shot and `exe+0x22B770` adds 100 score. Contact: `exe+0x1FF4F0(player, amount)`, called from `exe+0x213D70` among others. `exe+0x207A50` adds the score (section 2). Source: Ghidra 2026-11-08 (TODO 5.2 stage 3). To verify: hook `exe+0x1FF340` and `exe+0x22B770` on a bench with a friend ship.
- **How a ship's place across the rail moves:** (L231): Confirmed: the place, see section 2 `exe+0xAEA1A0 +0x80`. `exe+0x1E8ED0()` returns that object (mode at +0x94). `exe+0x1FFE80(player)` turns the stick into a step that `exe+0x1E9B00` adds, stopping at x +-180, y +-140 for modes 0-3. `exe+0x1FD1E0` reads the stick only in state 2; in state 3 (the goal) it pulls the place back to the centre (not seen in a run). Source: Ghidra 2026-11-08 (TODO 5.2 stage 2). To verify: read +0x80 at a corner.
- **How Gummi flight objects update, get hit and die:** (L232): `exe+0x1E4D30` steps every `gb::OBJ` by type 0-6; `exe+0x1E2250` builds one from a 0x60-byte row by id. `gb::ENEMY`: +0x448 param row (+6 contact damage, +8 score), +0x450 i32 HP, +0x470 flags (bit 0 going down). `exe+0x214190(enemy, attack)` takes a hit (attack +0x18 damage). `exe+0x213D70` is its contact with the player; it and the friend's absorb `exe+0x22B770` are hooked but no run showed them running. `exe+0x207A50` caps the score at 99,999,990 and keeps the last digit, which `exe+0x207A90` raises (unless 9) while PLAYER +0x140C bit 0 is set; in one run that digit matched this count (section 2, `exe+0x72B130`). Source: Ghidra 2026-11-08 (TODO 5.2 stage 3). To verify: ram an enemy.
- **The flight's HP gauge and lock-on pips:** (L233): Confirmed: the gauge and the functions we call, see sections 1 and 2. `gb::MENU` is one static object at `exe+0x72C280`. Gauge state +0x188: `exe+0x2366B0` sets 1 (builds the sprites, then 2), 2 updates, `exe+0x2358B0` sets 3 (one fade-out). Ship values come from `*(exe+0xAF0540)`: HP PLAYER +0x578, max +0x57C (the ring shows 1 - HP / max). The pips read the `gb::LOCKON` at PLAYER +0xB90 (L235): count +0x71C (32 at most), state +0x708; a sight whose +0x34 == 1 lights a pip. Gauge +0x18C is its alpha, falling while the ship's place is past fixed x, y limits, likely behind the HUD. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-09 (TODO 5.2). To verify: read +0x188 and +0x18C on a bench.
- **Gummi sprites and circle masks:** (L234): Confirmed: the fields we use, see section 3. `exe+0x1F3460(exe+0x1F3270(), group, anim)` makes a `gb::SPRITE` (0x1D8 bytes, list `exe+0xAEB548`, next handle +0x1D4): +0x1BC z (`exe+0x1F3F10` sets x, y, z), +0x1C0 alpha, +0x1D0 flags (bit 0 skipped, bit 5 fading). A `gb::CIRCLE_MASK` sits at gauge +0x170 and +0x158: angle +0x10 (set by `exe+0x243250`, `exe+0x2431B0`), drawn by the thunk `exe+0x2358A0` to `exe+0x241D70`. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-09 (TODO 5.2). To verify: change a sprite's +0x1C0 and a mask's +0x10 on a bench.
- **Charged lasers, gb::LOCKON and gb::LOCKON_GUN:** (L235): Confirmed: the fields used, see section 3. One `gb::LOCKON` per player at PLAYER +0xB90 (constructor `exe+0x1FC170`, list `exe+0xAEF3E0`); +0x708: 0 idle, 1 locking, 2-3 firing, 4 back to 0. Part ids 0x1F6-0x1F8, 0x1FB, 0x26E go to `exe+0x203960`, which makes a `gb::LOCKON_GUN` (0x1C8 bytes, kind +0x92 0x44 / 0x46 / 0x54). Pips per group: `exe+0x1F72E0` (kind 0x54), `exe+0x1F7240` (+0x710), `exe+0x1F73E0` (+0x70C), `exe+0x1F71A0` (a part taking all 32). Gun vtable `exe+0x5B6118` +0x10 fires (`exe+0x232490`); its step `exe+0x1F0F00`: +0x9C bit 0 the fire request, +0x8C cooldown. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-09 (TODO 5.2). To verify: read +0x708 through a volley.
- **How a lock-on volley flies:** (L236): `exe+0x1FB610(gumiship, lockon)` and `exe+0x1FB6A0` walk a gumiship's parts (+0x98; +0x4C8 == 2 alive), `exe+0x205900(part, lockon)` a part's guns (+0x570). The laser is a `gb::PLAYER_HOMING` (0x1C0 bytes, vtable `exe+0x5B5DD8`, list `exe+0xAF91D8`); its collision `exe+0x223F60` with +0x1B5 = 1 (default) tests only its sight's enemy; kind 0x54 hits anything on its way. `exe+0x214190(enemy, attack)` is the damage (enemy +0x450 HP, attack +0x18 power). Sight step `exe+0x1F7BC0`: state 4 uses the reticle +0x28 with no null check. `gb::OBJ +0x1FC` bit 0 = gone. Enemies listed at `exe+0xAF7618`. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-09 (TODO 5.2). To verify: hook `exe+0x214190` during a volley.
- **A Gummi ship's hit:** (L237): `exe+0x1FF4F0(player, power)` applies a hit: it sets +0x140C bit 0 and the invincibility timer +0x5DC (callers: the enemy contact and three shot tests). The player's step `exe+0x1FD1E0` turns bit 0 into bit 1 while flying and plays effect 1; effect 0 is a wall bounce, 4 a looped one while +0x140C bit 2 is set (not known what it shows). Source: Ghidra 2026-11-09 (TODO 5.2). To verify: hook `exe+0x1FF4F0` on a bench and read +0x5DC.
- **Gummi orbs and the orb level:** (L238): `exe+0x214570` (section 1) reads score orbs at +0xD and heal orbs at +0xE of the row at enemy +0x448, doing nothing while +0x470 bit 11 is set; each orb is `exe+0x20D560(enemy, kind)` (0 score, 2 heal), which rolls the random number (L240). `exe+0x214350` returns at once with +0x470 bit 9 (0x200); with enemy +0x21C non-zero it tries a treasure, dropping orbs only if there is none. Pickup `exe+0x20E5D0(orb)`, kind orb +0x95. `exe+0x207C30` counts score orbs: `exe+0x72B2C0` toward the next level (10), `exe+0x72B2C4` the level (max 0x1E), `exe+0x72B2C8` the best. `exe+0x207A50` adds nothing while byte `exe+0x72B134` is set. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-09 (TODO 5.2). To verify: count orbs on a bench.
- **Portraits in the Gummi menu's HD textures:** (L239): Confirmed: see section 4.
- **The game's random number in flight code:** (L240): `exe+0x783CA0` (section 2) is one u32, stepped with `x = x * 0x10DCD` written inline at each use; about 70 rolls are in the flight code (`exe+0x1E3940` to `exe+0x23FF70`). No separate stream for enemies was seen; what a roll decides for a given enemy kind isn't read yet. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-09 (TODO 5.2b). To verify: log `exe+0x783CA0` on a bench around one enemy kind's rolls.
- **What moves a Gummi boss:** (L241): Confirmed: each boss follows one of a few fixed paths (F465), picked by its script (F468, `exe+0x7535C0`, L244 to L246), not the ship's position (F466); showing the host's copy covers only its moves, place, HP and aim (F467). `gb::ENEMY` updates via `exe+0x212830`, which only calls `exe+0x213370`. Its motion player (+0xB8) queues four motions, each with a stored function; `exe+0x226AD0`, `exe+0x226B10`, `exe+0x227740`, `exe+0x2295E0` look like those, the thin ones script commands. Source: Ghidra and benches 2026-11-09 (TODO 5.2b). To verify: hook the four and see which run from the queue.
- **A Gummi object's script:** (L244): `exe+0x1E52E0(obj)` runs the script thread at `gb::OBJ +0x3A0` unless +0x1F8 bit 0; with +0x1FC bit 2 it first starts trigger 0 (OBJ_INIT) and 1 (OBJ_MAIN), then clears it, all inside the enemy's step (`exe+0x213370`). Command tables at `exe+0x753490`, 8 bytes a slot: slots 3 and 9 are empty in the file and flight scripts call table 3, so it's probably filled at run time. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-10 (TODO 5.2b). To verify: read slot 3 in flight.
- **The Gummi object table 50gumientry.bin:** (L245): `50gumientry.bin`: 8 bytes of header (3, then 277 rows), then 0x60-byte rows sorted by id: +0 u32 kind, +4 u32 row kind, +8 model name, then the motion file. 0x57D `G_EX260_R` (`G_EX260.mset`), 0x57F `G_EX261_R`, 0x581 `G_EX262_R`, 0x587 `G_EX300_R`. Source: the game file, read with a scratch script 2026-11-10 (TODO 5.2b). To verify: spawn these ids on a bench and see these models.
- **The red Gummi boss's script:** (L246): `obj/G_EX260_R.mdlx` holds script `g_ex260` (entry `g_ex`, Bdx). At the start it rolls `random_get(4)` into work +20, the attack index; each attack moves it on 1, 2, 3, 0: index 0 plays 202 with 203, 204, 0 queued; 1 plays 205; 2 plays 201; 3 plays 206 (the order of F466). That first roll is the only one the attack order needs. State 6 ends an attack when `syscall 3, 39` reads 0. No branch reads HP, a hit or the ship, only whether each arm is alive and the going-down flag; with no arm alive it goes to state 7, the only place with motion 232. The arms have no script. Source: decoded with OpenKH's `OpenKh.Command.Bdxio`, 2026-11-10 (TODO 5.2b). To verify: log work +20 at the fight's start.
- **How a Gummi enemy shot picks its target:** (L247): Confirmed: the aim (bit 6), see section 1 `kShotAim`. Bit 13 starts the shot at our ship; bit 4 spreads it on the flight's random number; bit 7 aims at gun +0x28 in the rail's frame (`exe+0x1E8ED0` returns `exe+0xAEA1A0`), the same in both games. Callers: `exe+0x1F0F00` (a beam, each tick), `exe+0x1F1110` (a fire), `exe+0x1F6480` / `exe+0x1F67A0` (a chaser's first step), `exe+0x231DB0`, `exe+0x232490`. `exe+0x1FD150` only ever returns our own ship (`*(exe+0xAF0540)`). Source: Ghidra 2026-11-10 (TODO 5.2b). To verify: log the start and aim of bit 13 and bit 7 shots on a bench.
- **Gummi flight script commands, table 3:** (L248): Table 3 is `exe+0x72F1A0`, 16 bytes a row (function, flags: low bits the argument count, 0x40000000 returns a value), put in slot 3 by `exe+0x1F9AA0`, cleared by `exe+0x1F9A80`. `3,35` `exe+0x226AD0` play now; `3,36` `exe+0x226B10`; `3,37` `exe+0x226B50` queue; `3,38` `exe+0x226BA0` reads the ended bit (obj +0xD8 bit 1); `3,39` `exe+0x226BE0` reads obj +0xD0; `3,8` `exe+0x227EC0` fires a gun group (guns at obj +0x478); `3,44` `exe+0x2289C0` returns our ship; `3,178` sets the motion speed (+0x3EC). Source: Ghidra 2026-11-10 (TODO 5.2b). To verify: hook `3,35` and `3,37` on a bench and log the motions.
- **The Gummi motion player:** (L249): Confirmed: calling the setter directly restarts the clock a queued pop waits on, see F469. `gb::OBJ +0xB8`: +0x18 script's motion, +0x1C setter's motion, +0x20 flags (bit 1 ended, a one-step pulse), +0x24 length, +0x28 time, +0x38 queue write, +0x3C read, 4 rows of 0x18 at +0x40 (motion, blend, start, stored function). The setter `exe+0x217390` writes -1 over +0x18 and +0x1C, replaces length and time, then writes only +0x1C. Play now (`exe+0x216DC0`) empties the queue, writes +0x18. The tick (`exe+0x216220`) pops when time reaches length; empty, it only sets ended. Step order: tick, script, the mod's `FollowHost`. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-10 (TODO 5.2b). To verify: log +0x38 and +0x3C.
- **Gummi enemy guns:** (L250): Built by `3,6` / `3,7`: +0x90 group, +0x91 tag (`3,42`, an arm's number or 100), +0x9C bit 0 fire, bit 4 spread (`3,25`), bit 6 aim at the ship (`3,27`). Their ticks look like separate tasks (`exe+0x1F0B60` registers them), outside the object step. Shots come from a pool of 0xB0-byte records (`exe+0x21E660`), not `gb::OBJ`, so they run no script. Object factory `exe+0x1E2250`: row kind 3 is type 2, 4 type 3, 8 type 1, 13 type 4 (`G_EX100`, `G_EX110`, `G_EX120`, chasers the guns make, with scripts and no rolls). Source: Ghidra 2026-11-10 (TODO 5.2b). To verify: hook `exe+0x1F0B60` and see the gun ticks run outside the object step.
- **Gummi enemy files:** (L252): `gumibattle/enemyparam.bin`: 0x40-byte rows, HP at +0x20 (matches 2800 and 14000). Of 122 `G_E*` models 95 have a script and 46 of those roll; `G_B*` none. 16 scripts read the ship through `3,44` (`G_EC130`, `G_EC140`, `G_EX220`, `G_EX250_0`, `G_EX270`, `G_EX900_COMBAT`, `G_EX900_FLIGHT`), unread. `G_EX260.mset` motion lengths: 0 160, 45 200, 201 240, 202 100, 203 120, 204 60, 205 150, 206 320, 232 270, 233 200 (they match the logs). Source: game files read with OpenKH and a scratch script, 2026-11-10 (TODO 5.2b). To verify: compare more kinds' HP with a bench read.
- **Gummi enemy shot kinds and who reads the ship:** (L253): Confirmed: the newest homing shot `*(exe+0xAF8410)`, see section 2. `exe+0x1F0C50` fires by gun kind (+0x92): 0x25 `gb::ENEMY_HOMING` (vtable `exe+0x5B5CE0`, target ship +0x98 from `exe+0x1FD150`), 0x26-0x28 the chasers. `exe+0x1E6BA0(ship, out)` reads ship +8, so swapping the ship's place covers bits 6 and 13 of `exe+0x1F1B20`. The homing update `exe+0x221450` steers toward the target's place unless +0xA4 is set, which its hit test `exe+0x221650` (like the other kinds', against this game's own ship only) sets within 400 units of our ship. Parts: `gb::PLAYER` +0x488, `gb::FRIEND` +0x440 (`exe+0x1FAB30(friend + 0x440, slot + 1)`). Source: Ghidra 2026-11-10 (TODO 5.2b). To verify: log +0xA4.

### Link, DLL runtime and crashes

- **Friend crash after a Drive in Data Xemnas's second part:** (L31): In one two-PC run the friend's game, started 50 s before and joined mid-fight, crashed right after 'Drive into form 3' at `exe+0x3C859E` (`call [rax+0x28]`, a broken pointer), called from the per-frame actor update (return address `exe+0x422421`). Not reproduced. Source: crash, two-PC run 2026-10-17. To verify: Drive in that fight on a bench.
- **An exception in the mission task at a Game Over:** (L38): In one two-PC run, after the friend chose Continue like the host and a move to 0x08/0x01 door 0x35 was refused (door lock), its game faulted at `exe+0x3A380B` reading 0x8 (rcx 0). Our mission task hook caught it and dropped the ending orders, and the game went on. Not looked into. Source: two-PC log 2026-10-15. To verify: repeat that Continue on a bench.
- **Volpestyle has no crown or cutscene appearance work:** (L75): Volpestyle's repo never mentions the crown and has no cutscene appearance work: they don't edit cutscenes, and clients wait behind an overlay during the host's. Source: another project (Volpestyle, main c2c76e6), read 2026-10-11. To verify: search their repo again after a pull.
- **The game's Steam ID read:** (L76): `exe+0x14D1E0` returns the player's CSteamID: a cached one (`exe+0x8CC44C`) if valid, else GetSteamID on the ISteamUser* from import `exe+0x57BC78` (likely SteamInternal_ContextInit). The save path builder `exe+0x103E40` calls it. When the game's Steam didn't start, that pointer is null and the game crashes at `exe+0x14D275` at boot (not the mod). Source: the friend's crash + disassembly 2026-10-10. To verify: start the game without Steam.
- **Steam networking:** (L77): Confirmed: the exports we use, the game's Steam session, NetworkingMessages send/receive, rich presence and Join Game, see the 'Steam link' row. Strings only in `steam_api64.dll`, untried: `SteamAPI_ISteamMatchmaking_CreateLobby`, `SteamAPI_ISteamFriends_InviteUserToGame`, the overlay invite dialog. Volpestyle: relay-only P2P sockets on the game's Steam. Source: file read + Volpestyle docs 2026-10-10. To verify: try them with two Steam accounts.
- **The game's frame step:** (L180): Float `exe+0x717480` is subtracted each update from timers kept in seconds (the party regen timer `exe+0x3C3150`, L22, reset from `*(exe+0x2AE5760)+0x168`): the game's logic is time-stepped. Not read live yet. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-01. To verify: read it live at two frame rates.
- **Frame time `exe+0x717480`:** (L251): `exe+0x717480` (f32) is what `trap_frametime` (`exe+0x41CA50`) reads and the motion tick uses; written in `exe+0x154740` and `exe+0x1548E0`, capped at 6.0, so a hitch longer than 6 frames loses game time in that game only. Source: Ghidra 2026-11-10. To verify: read it live during a hitch.
- **The RTN meeting lookup:** (L311): Confirmed: our made `_RTN`s trigger the crash, see F546. The RTN step `exe+0x4179E0` (from the RTN update `exe+0x416270`) calls `exe+0x417FD0(a, b)` both ways for the first other actor it touches with another serial and +0x6C8 bit 3; it reads a+0x9E8 (the spawn group) with no null check. Source: Ghidra and crash registers, 2026-11-12. To verify: read +0x9E8 of the made actor at the fault.

## 7. Address index

## Gaps (what's left of the restructuring)
- Fold the field facts into section 3, one table per structure, one row per field, only fields confirmed live (a Ghidra-only field stays in its lead). The about 150 findings and leads written as compact field lists are the first candidates.
- Sort section 6: promote what a bench confirmed into sections 1 to 5 (with the run that shows it). On 2026-11-10 no lead was wholly confirmed but not yet moved: L91, L102 and L239 are pointers, L140 holds the Ghidra detail its section 1 row points to, and the leads that start with 'Confirmed:' are confirmed only in part.
- Section 1 rows for the code's functions: 'First bytes the DLL checks' (2026-11-10) lists every hooked or checked address, and every row whose address the code names carries that name, but some 200 function constants of the code have no section 1 row yet, only mentions in findings and leads; add them as those are rewritten.
- The short rewrite (2026-11-10) left out the Ghidra sub-calls, vtables and return addresses the code doesn't use (about 330, most from the Gummi leads; 1393 addresses in the file before, 1208 after). Each is in the history under its entry's number.
- 35 addresses the code names were dropped from one entry each in the short rewrite but stay in another entry or in sections 1 to 4 (L48 and L58 lost the most); each is in the history under its entry's number.
- Seven code constants have no entry yet: `exe+0x623BAC` (`kBlend`, story_sync.cpp); the title menu's `exe+0xB1D5E0`, `exe+0xB1D5E4`, `exe+0xB1D5F0` (`kAction`, `kCursor`, `kRowCount`, title_menu.cpp); the return addresses `exe+0x41F768`, `exe+0x42EE60` (`kRecycleReturn`, `kCullingCallReturn`, aggro.cpp, on one line, which check_docs misses); the .text end `exe+0x579000` (`kTextEnd`, crash_log.cpp, from the PE headers). Stale section 7 links hid the first four until the index was rebuilt from sections 5 and 6 on 2026-11-10.
- Nothing in the repo rebuilds section 7's 'Mentioned in', and check_docs counts a row that points at entries as an entry, so a stale link hides a code constant no entry names. Rebuild the column in check_docs (or stop trusting it) so it can't drift again.
- Four code comments still cite findings by title (companion_heal.cpp, grab_sync.cpp, shared_move.cpp, skip_sync.cpp): give them F numbers in a code session.
- `tools/check_docs.py` (built 2026-10-22) warns about code constants with no entry here, long cells in sections 1 to 4, broken tables and the size budget (600 KB since 2026-11-10: a growth alarm; the per-entry limits are the real rule). It misses constants that share a line (`kA = 0x..., kB = 0x...;`) and new hooks missing from the first-bytes table. Since 2026-11-10 it checks sections 5 and 6 too (a lead's form and 'Source:' / 'To verify:', cut marks, run details, the 600 / 700 layout limits), but not the 300 / 450 limit for an entry that isn't a layout. Later, `offsets.hpp` generated from a table so the two cannot drift.
